# Israel

This article is about the country. For the region, see [Land of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_of_Israel). For the biblical figure also named Israel, see [Jacob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob). For other uses, see [Israel (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_\(disambiguation\)).

**Israel**,[^cite_note-29] officially the **State of Israel**,[^cite_note-30] is a country in the [Southern Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Levant) region of [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia). It is [bordered](12999306.md) by [Lebanon](17771.md) to the north, [Syria](7515849.md) to the northeast, [Jordan](7515964.md) to the east, and [Egypt](8087628.md) to the southwest. [Israel's western coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_coastal_plain) lies on the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md), its southern tip reaches the [Red Sea](26191.md), and to the east is [Earth's lowest point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extremes_on_Earth) near the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea). [Jerusalem](16043.md) is the government seat and [proclaimed capital](4024423.md),[^cite_note-31] while [Tel Aviv](31453.md) is Israel's [largest urban area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Dan) and economic centre.&#x20;

The [Land of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_of_Israel), also called [Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_\(region\)) or the [Holy Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Land), was home to the ancient [Canaanites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan) and later the kingdoms of [Israel and Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Israel_and_Judah) and [Hasmonean Judea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasmonean_Judea). Located near [continental crossroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levantine_corridor), its [demographics shifted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Palestine_\(region\)) under various empires. 19th-century Jewish nationalism, alongside [European antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_antisemitism), fuelled the [Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist) movement for a [Jewish homeland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_homeland) in Palestine, then an Ottoman region with an overwhelming Arab majority.[^cite_note-32] The movement gained [British](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Empire) support with the 1917 [Balfour Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balfour_Declaration). After [World War I](4764461.md), Britain occupied the region and established [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine). British rule and [Jewish immigration](351513.md) in the leadup to the Holocaust intensified [Arab-Jewish tensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercommunal_conflict_in_Mandatory_Palestine) with Arab opposition to Zionism driven largely by concerns of displacement and dispossession.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] The 1947 [UN Partition Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine) proposed separate Arab and Jewish states. The Jewish leadership accepted the plan while the Palestinian Arab leadership rejected it, arguing it violated the principle of [self-determination](29269.md) and constituted Western colonial interference.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-\:0-36] [Civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947%E2%80%931948_civil_war_in_Mandatory_Palestine) followed, and Israel [declared independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence) on 14 May 1948, shortly before the end of the [mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandate_for_Palestine). The conflict subsequently escalated with the [intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War) of [Arab states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world).&#x20;

The [1949 armistice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949_Armistice_Agreements) left Israel controlling more territory than had been allocated to it under the UN partition plan.[^cite_note-Britannica-37] No independent Palestinian Arab state was established, leaving [Gaza under Egyptian control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_the_Gaza_Strip_by_the_United_Arab_Republic) and the [West Bank ruled by Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_annexation_of_the_West_Bank).[^cite_note-Britannica-37][^cite_note-38][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFischbach200826–27-39] Most [Palestinian Arabs](23267.md) [fled or were expelled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestinian_expulsion_and_flight) during the [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESlater202081–92,_350-40][^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42] while Israeli independence prompted [antisemitism in the Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Arab_world) and [Jewish exodus therefrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_exodus_from_the_Muslim_world), mainly to Israel.[^cite_note-\:05-43][^cite_note-\:22-44] After the 1967 [Six-Day War](29328.md), Israel occupied the West Bank, Gaza and Egyptian [Sinai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_the_Sinai_Peninsula), and annexed [East Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_annexation_of_East_Jerusalem) and the Syrian [Golan Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights_Law). Peace treaties were signed with [Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty) in 1979 (the Sinai being returned in 1982) and [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_peace_treaty) in 1994. The 1993 [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords) introduced limited [Palestinian self-rule](24093.md) in the West Bank and Gaza, while the 2020 [Abraham Accords](64883396.md) [normalised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_normalization) ties with more Arab states, but the [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md) persists. Israel's [illegal occupation of the Palestinian territories](2781576.md) has drawn international criticism, with most Middle East experts arguing that Israel's treatment of Palestinians in the West Bank in relation to [Israeli settlers](15123.md) constitutes a system of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_apartheid).[^cite_note-sf-45][^cite_note-\:9-46] After the [Hamas](13913.md)-led [October 7 attacks](74998317.md), Israel began committing [genocide against Palestinians in Gaza](75668427.md).[^cite_note-47] Israel and its allies, including the [United States](3434750.md), [deny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_denial) that Israel's actions constitute genocide.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50]&#x20;

The [Basic Laws of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Laws_of_Israel) establish the [Knesset](74656.md) as a [proportionally](23298.md) elected [parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system). It shapes the [government](5006115.md), led by the [prime minister](24898.md), and elects the largely ceremonial [president](153049.md).[^cite_note-cnn-51] Israel has one of the Middle East's largest economies,[^cite_note-52] [one of Asia's highest living standards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_in_Asia_and_Oceania_by_Human_Development_Index), and globally ranks [25th in nominal GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) and [14th in nominal GDP per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)_per_capita).[^cite_note-IMFWEO.IL-26][^cite_note-53] One of the world's most [technologically advanced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Israel) countries,[^cite_note-54] Israel allocates a larger share of [its economy](14690.md) to research and development [than any other state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_by_research_and_development_spending)[^cite_note-55][^cite_note-56] and is believed to [possess nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_Israel). The only country with a [revived](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revival_of_the_Hebrew_language) official language, [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Hebrew), the [culture of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Israel) combines [Jewish traditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_culture) with [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_culture) influences.&#x20;

## Etymology

Further information: [Israel (name)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_\(name\)) and [Names of the Levant § Israel and Judea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_Levant#Israel_and_Judea)

The names [Land of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_of_Israel) and Children of Israel have historically been used to refer to the biblical [Kingdom of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Israel_\(united_monarchy\)) and the entire [Jewish](25955086.md) people respectively. The [name *Israel*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_\(name\)) (Hebrew: *Yīsrāʾēl*; [Septuagint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septuagint) [Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek_language): Ἰσραήλ, *Israēl*, "[El (God)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_\(deity\)) persists/rules") refers to the patriarch [Jacob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob) who, according to the [Hebrew Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible), was given the name after he successfully [wrestled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_wrestling_with_the_angel) with the [Angel of the Lord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_of_the_Lord).[^cite_note-57] The earliest known archaeological artefact to mention the word *Israel* as a collective is the [Merneptah Stele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merneptah_Stele) of [ancient Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt) (dated to the late-13th century BCE).[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-NollMerneptah-59][^cite_note-ThompsonMerneptah-60][^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63]&#x20;

Under the [British Mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandate_for_Palestine) (1920–1948), the entire region was known as *Palestine*.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-65] Upon [establishment in 1948](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence), the country formally adopted the name *State of Israel* ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): מְדִינַת יִשְׂרָאֵל, [*_Medīnat Yisrā'el_*](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/93/He-Medinat_Israel.ogg/He-Medinat_Israel.ogg.mp3)<sup>ⓘ</sup> [\[mediˈnat jisʁaˈʔel\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Hebrew); [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): دَوْلَة إِسْرَائِيل, *_Dawlat Isrāʼīl_*, [\[dawlat ʔisraːˈʔiːl\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Arabic)) after other [proposed names](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence#Name) including *[Land of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_of_Israel)* (*Eretz Israel*), *Ever* (from ancestor [Eber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eber)), *[Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion)*, and *[Judea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judea)*, were considered but rejected.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] The name *Israel* was suggested by [David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion) and passed by a vote of 6–3.[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69] In the early weeks after establishment, the government chose the term *[Israeli](181296.md)* to denote a citizen of the state.[^cite_note-70]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Israel)

Further information: [History of Israel (1948–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Israel_\(1948%E2%80%93present\))

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Israeli history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Israeli_history).

### Prehistory

Further information: [Prehistory of the Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistory_of_the_Levant)

The [Ubeidiya prehistoric site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubeidiya_prehistoric_site) in northern Israel shows the [presence of archaic humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_expansions_of_hominins_out_of_Africa) around 1.5 million years ago.[^cite_note-71] The second-oldest evidence of [anatomically modern humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatomically_modern_human) [outside Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recent_African_origin_of_modern_humans) is a 200,000-year-old fossil from [Misliya Cave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misliya_Cave) on Mount Carmel.[^cite_note-72] The [Natufian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natufian_culture) (c. 10,000 BCE) may be linked to the [Proto-Afroasiatic language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Afroasiatic_language)[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] and is notable for adopting [sedentism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sedentism) before the [advent of agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origins_of_agriculture_in_West_Asia) and the [Neolithic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic_Revolution).[^cite_note-75]&#x20;

### Bronze and Iron Ages

Further information: [Ancient Israel and Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Israel_and_Judah)



[Tel Megiddo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Megiddo), the ruins of a [Canaanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan) and later [Israelite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israelites) city

Early references to "[Canaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan)" and "Canaanites" appear in ancient [Near Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Near_East) and [Egyptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt) texts (c. 2000 BCE); these populations were structured as politically independent [city-states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-state).[^cite_note-76][^cite_note-77] During the [Late Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Bronze_Age) (1550–1200 BCE), large parts of Canaan formed [vassal states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vassal_state) of the [New Kingdom of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Kingdom_of_Egypt).[^cite_note-78] As a result of the [Late Bronze Age collapse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Bronze_Age_collapse), Canaan fell into chaos, and Egyptian control over the region collapsed.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80] Ancestors of the [Israelites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israelites) are thought to have included [ancient Semitic-speaking peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Semitic-speaking_peoples) native to this area.[^cite_note-Miller1986-81]<sup>: 78–79 </sup> Modern archaeological accounts suggest that the Israelites and their culture branched out of the Canaanite peoples through [the settlement of the central highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israelite_highland_settlement) and the development of a distinct [monolatristic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monolatry)—and later [monotheistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monotheism)—religion centered on [Yahweh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahweh).[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83] They spoke an archaic form of [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language), known as [Biblical Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biblical_Hebrew).[^cite_note-84] Around the same time, the [Philistines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philistines) settled on the southern [coastal plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_coastal_plain).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKillebrew2005230-85][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShahin20056-86]&#x20;



The [Yavne-Yam ostracon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yavne-Yam_ostracon), a [Paleo-Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleo-Hebrew_alphabet) inscription documenting administration in [Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Judah)

Most modern scholars agree that the [Exodus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Exodus) narrative in the [Torah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torah) and [Old Testament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Testament) did not take place as depicted; however, some elements of these traditions do have [historical roots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historicity_of_the_Bible).[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] There is debate about the earliest existence of the [Kingdoms of Israel and Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_ancient_Israel_and_Judah) and their extent and power. While it is unclear if there was a [United Kingdom of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Israel_\(united_monarchy\)),[^cite_note-lipschits-89][^cite_note-Kuhrtp438-90] historians and archaeologists agree that the northern [Kingdom of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Israel_\(Samaria\)) existed by ca. 900 BCE[^cite_note-Finkelstein-91]<sup>: 169–195 </sup> and the [Kingdom of Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Judah) by ca. 850 BCE.[^cite_note-Finkelstein,_Israel,_(2020)-92][^cite_note-Pitcher-93] The Kingdom of Israel was the more prosperous of the two and soon developed into a regional power, with a capital at [Samaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaria_\(ancient_city\));[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-\:04-95][^cite_note-96] during the [Omride dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omrides), it controlled [Samaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaria), [Galilee](12639.md), the upper [Jordan Valley](12105764.md), the [plain of Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharon_plain) and large parts of [Transjordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transjordan_\(region\)).[^cite_note-\:04-95] The Kingdom of Israel [was conquered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samerina) around 720 BCE by the [Neo-Assyrian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Assyrian_Empire).[^cite_note-Broshi_2001_174-97] The Kingdom of Judah, under [Davidic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davidic_line) rule with its capital in [Jerusalem](16043.md), later became a [client state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Client_state) of first the Neo-Assyrian Empire and then the [Neo-Babylonian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Babylonian_Empire). It is estimated that [the region's population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Palestine_\(region\)) was around 400,000 in the [Iron Age II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age_II).[^cite_note-\:42-98] In 587/6 BCE, following a [revolt in Judah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judah%27s_revolts_against_Babylon), King [Nebuchadnezzar II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nebuchadnezzar_II) [besieged and destroyed Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jerusalem_\(587_BC\)) and [Solomon's Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomon%27s_Temple),[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] dissolved the kingdom and [exiled much of the Judean elite to Babylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babylonian_captivity).[^cite_note-101]&#x20;

### Classical antiquity

Further information: [Second Temple period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Temple_period)



[Hasmonean coin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasmonean_coinage) of [Antigonus II Mattathias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antigonus_II_Mattathias), depicting the [Temple menorah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_menorah)

After [capturing Babylon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Babylon) in 539 BCE, [Cyrus the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyrus_the_Great), founder of the [Achaemenid Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achaemenid_Empire), issued [a proclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edict_of_Cyrus) allowing the exiled Judean population to return.[^cite_note-rennert-102][^cite_note-103] The construction of the [Second Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Temple) was completed c. 520 BCE.[^cite_note-rennert-102] The Achaemenids ruled the region as the province of [Yehud Medinata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehud_Medinata).[^cite_note-Grabbe355-104] In 332 BCE, [Alexander the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_the_Great) conquered the region as part of his [campaign against the Achaemenid Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wars_of_Alexander_the_Great). After his death, the area [was controlled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenistic_Palestine) by the [Ptolemaic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemaic_Kingdom) and [Seleucid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seleucid_Empire) empires as a part of [Coele-Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coele-Syria). Under the Hellenistic kingdoms, ongoing [Hellenisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenization) generated cultural tensions among the Jewish population that culminated under [Antiochus IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiochus_IV_Epiphanes), whose decrees outlawed Jewish practices and triggered the [Maccabean Revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabean_Revolt) in 167 BCE. The revolt weakened Seleucid control over Judea; by 142/141 BCE the [Hasmoneans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasmonean_dynasty) had secured autonomy and soon established an [independent Jewish kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasmonean_Judea) that, in the late 2nd–early 1st century BCE, expanded into neighbouring territories.[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] The [Hasmonean civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasmonean_civil_war) ended with the [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Republic) [siege of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jerusalem_\(63_BC\)) in 63 BCE.&#x20;



View of the [Masada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masada) fortress overlooking the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea), which is the location of a [1st-century Roman siege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Masada)

In 37 BCE, [Herod the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herod_the_Great) was installed as a [dynastic vassal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herodian_dynasty) of [Rome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome) following the [Roman–Parthian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman%E2%80%93Parthian_Wars). In 6 CE, the area was annexed as the [Roman province of Judaea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaea_\(Roman_province\)); tensions with Roman rule led to a series of [Jewish–Roman wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_wars), resulting in widespread destruction. The [First Jewish–Roman War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_War) (66–73 CE) resulted in the [destruction of Jerusalem and the Second Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Jerusalem_\(70_CE\)) and a sizable portion of the population being killed or displaced.[^cite_note-\:8-108] A second uprising known as the [Bar Kokhba revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_Kokhba_revolt) (132–136 CE) initially allowed the Jews to form an independent state, but the Romans brutally crushed the rebellion, devastating and depopulating Judea's countryside.[^cite_note-\:8-108][^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110][^cite_note-\:02-111][^cite_note-112] Jerusalem was rebuilt as a [Roman colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonia_\(Roman\)) ([Aelia Capitolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aelia_Capitolina)), and the province of Judea was renamed [Syria Palaestina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syria_Palaestina).[^cite_note-H.H._Ben-Sasson,_1976,_page_334-113][^cite_note-Ariel_Lewin_p._33-114] Jews were expelled from the districts surrounding Jerusalem.[^cite_note-\:02-111][^cite_note-115] Nevertheless, there was a continuous small Jewish presence, and Galilee became its religious center.[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117]&#x20;

### Late antiquity and the medieval period

Further information: [Byzantine Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Palestine), [Bilad al-Sham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilad_al-Sham), and [Kingdom of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Jerusalem)



3rd-century [Kfar Bar'am synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Bar%27am_synagogue) in the Galilee

[^cite_note-118]

During the [Byzantine period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Palestine), [Early Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Christianity) displaced [Roman paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_ancient_Rome) in the 4th century CE, with [Constantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantine_the_Great) embracing and promoting the Christian religion and [Theodosius I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodosius_I) making it [the state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_as_the_Roman_state_religion). A series of laws were passed that discriminated against Jews and Judaism, and Jews were persecuted by both the church and the authorities.[^cite_note-\:3-119] Many Jews had emigrated to flourishing [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_diaspora) communities,[^cite_note-\:1-120] while locally there was both Christian immigration and local conversion. By the middle of the 5th century, there was a Christian majority.[^cite_note-CHJ2-121][^cite_note-122] Towards the end of the 5th century, [Samaritan revolts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samaritan_revolts) erupted, continuing until the late 6th century and resulting in a large decrease in the Samaritan population.[^cite_note-123] After the [Sasanian conquest of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasanian_conquest_of_Jerusalem) and the short-lived [Jewish revolt against Heraclius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_revolt_against_Heraclius) in 614 CE, the Byzantine Empire [reconsolidated control of the area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine%E2%80%93Sasanian_War_of_602%E2%80%93628) in 628.[^cite_note-124]&#x20;

In 634–641 CE, the [Rashidun Caliphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashidun_Caliphate) [conquered the Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_conquest_of_the_Levant).[^cite_note-\:1-120][^cite_note-\:102-125][^cite_note-\:Ellenblum20102-126] Caliph [Umar ibn al-Khattab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umar_ibn_al-Khattab) (r. 634–644) lifted the Christian ban on Jews entering [Jerusalem](16043.md) and permitted them to worship there.[^cite_note-127] Over the next six centuries, control of the region transferred between the [Umayyad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umayyad_Caliphate), [Abbasid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbasid_Caliphate), and [Fatimid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatimid_Caliphate) caliphates, and subsequently the [Seljuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seljuk_dynasty) and [Ayyubid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayyubid_dynasty) dynasties.[^cite_note-MosheGil2-128] The population drastically decreased during the following several centuries, dropping from an estimated 1 million during Roman and Byzantine periods to about 300,000 by the early [Ottoman period](22278.md), and there was steady [Arabisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabization) and [Islamisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamization).[^cite_note-auto-129][^cite_note-\:42-98][^cite_note-\:102-125][^cite_note-\:Ellenblum20102-126][^cite_note-\:Broshi1979-130] The end of the 11th century brought the [Crusades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusades), [papally](23056.md)-sanctioned incursions of Christian crusaders intent on wresting Jerusalem and the [Holy Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Land) from Muslim control and establishing [crusader states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusader_states).[^cite_note-131] The Ayyubids pushed back the crusaders before Muslim rule was fully restored by the [Mamluk sultans of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamluk_Sultanate_\(Cairo\)) in 1291.[^cite_note-GudrunKramer-132]&#x20;

### Modern period and the emergence of Zionism

Main articles: [Ottoman Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Palestine), [Old Yishuv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Yishuv), and [Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism)



Jews at the [Western Wall](75225.md) in the 1870s, a holy site in [Judaism](15624.md)

In 1516, the Ottoman Empire conquered the region and ruled it as part of [Ottoman Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Syria).[^cite_note-\:7-133][^cite_note-auto-129] Two violent incidents took place against Jews, the [1517 Safed attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1517_Safed_attacks) and the [1517 Hebron attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1517_Hebron_attacks), after the Turkish Ottomans ousted the [Mamluks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamluk) during the [Ottoman–Mamluk War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Mamluk_War_\(1516%E2%80%931517\)).[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-JudaicaStudies1999-135] Under the Ottoman Empire, the Levant was fairly cosmopolitan, with religious freedoms for [Christians, Muslims, and Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_of_the_Book). In 1561 the [Ottoman sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suleiman_the_Magnificent) invited [Sephardic Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardic_Jews) escaping the [Spanish Inquisition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Inquisition) to settle in and rebuild the city of [Tiberias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiberias).[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-alfassa2-137]&#x20;

Under the Ottoman Empire's [millet system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millet_\(Ottoman_Empire\)), Christians and Jews were considered *[dhimmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhimmi)* ("protected") under [Ottoman law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_law) in exchange for loyalty to the state and payment of the *[jizya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jizya)* tax.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139] Non-Muslim Ottoman subjects faced geographic and lifestyle restrictions, though these were not always enforced.[^cite_note-autogenerated1-140][^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] The millet system organised non-Muslims into autonomous communities on the basis of religion.[^cite_note-\:4-143]&#x20;



The [First Zionist Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Zionist_Congress) (1897) in [Basel](4911.md), Switzerland

The [concept of an eventual return to Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Return_to_Zion) remained a symbol within religious Jewish belief which emphasised that their return should be determined by [Divine Providence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divine_providence_in_Judaism) rather than human action.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvineri2017-144] The Jewish population of Palestine from the Ottoman rule to the beginning of the Zionist movement, known as the [Old Yishuv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Yishuv), comprised a minority and fluctuated in size. During the 16th century, Jewish communities struck roots in the [Four Holy Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Holy_Cities)—Jerusalem, Tiberias, [Hebron](38577.md), and [Safed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safed)—and in 1697, Rabbi Yehuda Hachasid led 1,500 Jews to Jerusalem.[^cite_note-145] A 1660 [Druze revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_power_struggle_\(1658%E2%80%931667\)#Lebanon_and_Galilee_campaign) against the Ottomans destroyed [Safed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1660_destruction_of_Safed) and [Tiberias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1660_destruction_of_Tiberias).[^cite_note-\:7-133] In the second half of the 18th century, Eastern European Jews who were [opponents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misnagdim) of [Hasidism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasidic_Judaism), known as the [Perushim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perushim), settled in Palestine.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147]&#x20;

In the late 18th century, local Arab Sheikh [Daher al-Umar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daher_al-Umar) created a de facto independent emirate in the Galilee. Ottoman attempts to subdue the sheikh failed. After Daher's death the Ottomans regained control of the area. In 1799, governor [Jazzar Pasha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazzar_Pasha) repelled an [assault on Acre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Acre_\(1799\)) by [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon)'s troops, prompting the French to abandon the Syrian campaign.[^cite_note-148] In 1834, a [revolt by Palestinian Arab peasants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peasants%27_revolt_in_Palestine) against Egyptian conscription and taxation policies under [Muhammad Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_of_Egypt) was suppressed; Muhammad Ali's army retreated and Ottoman rule was restored with British support in 1840.[^cite_note-149] The [Tanzimat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzimat) reforms were implemented across the Ottoman Empire.&#x20;

The first wave of modern Jewish migration to [Ottoman-ruled Palestine](498693.md), known as the [First Aliyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Aliyah), began in 1881, as Jews fled [pogroms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogrom) in Eastern Europe.[^cite_note-150] The 1882 [May Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Laws) increased economic discrimination against Jews, and restricted where they could live.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152] Antisemitism, pogroms and official policies in tsarist Russia led to the emigration of three million Jews in the years between 1882 and 1914, only 1% of whom went to Palestine. Those who went to Palestine were driven primarily by ideas of self-determination and Jewish identity, rather than as a response to pogroms or economic insecurity.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvineri2017-144] [Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism) emerged during this time as a political movement to reestablish a Jewish homeland in Palestine.[^cite_note-153] The [revival of the Hebrew language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revival_of_the_Hebrew_language), during which [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language) was adapted for modern use and gradually became the primary language of Jews in Palestine, also began.[^cite_note-154]&#x20;

The [Second Aliyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Aliyah) (1904–1914) began after the [Kishinev pogrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kishinev_pogrom); some 40,000 Jews settled in Palestine, although nearly half left eventually. Both the first and second waves of migrants were mainly [Orthodox Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Judaism).[^cite_note-155] The Second Aliyah included [Zionist socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Zionism) groups who established the *[kibbutz](106200.md)* movement based on the idea of establishing a separate Jewish economy based exclusively on Jewish labour.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERomano200330-157] Those of the Second Aliyah who became leaders of the [Yishuv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yishuv) in the coming decades believed that the Jewish settler economy should not depend on Arab labour. This would be a dominant source of antagonism with the Arab population, with the new Yishuv's nationalist ideology overpowering its socialist one.[^cite_note-158] Though the immigrants of the Second Aliyah largely sought to create communal Jewish agricultural settlements, [Tel Aviv](31453.md) was established as the first planned Jewish town in 1909. Jewish armed militias emerged during this period, the first being [Bar-Giora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar-Giora_\(organization\)) in 1907; two years later, the larger [Hashomer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashomer) organisation was founded as its replacement.[^cite_note-159]&#x20;

### British Mandate for Palestine

Main article: [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine)

See also: [New Yishuv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Yishuv), [Intercommunal conflict in Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercommunal_conflict_in_Mandatory_Palestine), and [Jewish land purchase in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_land_purchase_in_Palestine)

[Chaim Weizmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaim_Weizmann)'s efforts to garner British support for the Zionist movement secured the [Balfour Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balfour_Declaration) of 1917,[^cite_note-160] stating Britain's support for the creation of a Jewish "[national home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_for_the_Jewish_people)" in Palestine.[^cite_note-macintyre-161][^cite_note-162] Balfour was aware of the developing principle of [self-determination](29269.md) and its implications for Palestine. In a letter to a leader of the Zionist movement in the United States, he wrote:

> The Powers had committed themselves to the Zionist programme which inevitably excluded numerical self-determination. Palestine presented a unique situation. We are dealing not with the wishes of an existing community but are consciously seeking to reconstitute a new community and definitely building for a numerical majority in the future.[^cite_note-163]&#x20;

Weizmann's interpretation of the declaration was that negotiations on the future of the country were to happen directly between Britain and the Jews, excluding Arabs. [Jewish-Arab relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%E2%80%93Jewish_relations) in Palestine deteriorated dramatically.[^cite_note-Avi_Shlaim-164]&#x20;



Tel Aviv in the 1930s

In 1918, the [Jewish Legion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Legion) assisted in the British [conquest of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_and_Palestine_campaign).[^cite_note-165] In 1920, the territory was divided between Britain and France under the [mandate system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Nations_mandate), and the British-administered area (including modern Israel) was named [Mandatory Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine).[^cite_note-GudrunKramer-132][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] When Western powers finalized the partition of the Middle East at the [San Remo Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Remo_conference), they did so against local opinion. Previously in 1919, the US-led [King-Crane Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King%E2%80%93Crane_Commission), the only comprehensive social survey of the era, had found overwhelming local opposition to the Zionist project and European mandatory rule.[^cite_note-168] The Arab opposition to British rule and Jewish immigration led to the [1920 Nebi Musa riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1920_Nebi_Musa_riots) and the formation of a Jewish militia known as the [Haganah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haganah) as an outgrowth of Hashomer, from which the [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) and [Lehi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_\(group\)) paramilitaries later split.[^cite_note-169] In 1922, the [League of Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Nations) granted Britain the [Mandate for Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandate_for_Palestine) under terms which included the Balfour Declaration with its promise to the Jews and with similar provisions regarding the Arab Palestinians.[^cite_note-170] The [population of the area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Palestine_\(region\)) was predominantly Arab and Muslim, with Jews accounting for about 11%[^cite_note-171] and Arab Christians about 9.5% of the population.[^cite_note-172]&#x20;

The [Third](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Aliyah) (1919–1923) and [Fourth Aliyahs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Aliyah) (1924–1929) brought an additional 100,000 Jews to Palestine. Following the [1929 Palestine riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1929_Palestine_riots), the [Hope Simpson Enquiry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hope_Simpson_Enquiry) (1930) documented that land purchases by the Jewish National Fund and other Zionist organizations had been leading to the eviction of Arab tenant farmers (fellahin) and creating a large economically disenfranchised class of landless Arabs. Hope Simpson made recommendations including limiting Jewish immigration based on the economic absorptive capacity of Palestine.[^cite_note-173]&#x20;

The [rise of Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler%27s_rise_to_power) and the increasing persecution of Jews in 1930s Europe led to the [Fifth Aliyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Aliyah), with an influx of 250,000 Jews. This was a cause of the [Arab revolt of 1936–39](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936%E2%80%931939_Arab_revolt_in_Palestine), which was suppressed by British security forces and Zionist militias. Several hundred British security personnel and Jews were killed; 5,032 Arabs were killed.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176] An estimated ten per cent of the adult male [Palestinian Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people) population was killed, wounded, imprisoned, or exiled.[^cite_note-177]&#x20;



"Jews and Arabs in Grim Struggle for Holy Land", article from 1938

In the 1930s, Britain began considering proposals to partition Palestine. Although some Zionists opposed partition, David Ben-Gurion supported it while regarding it as a temporary arrangement. In the summer of 1938, he stated that, after the establishment of the state and the formation of a large army, they would "abolish partition" and "expand to the whole of Palestine".[^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180]&#x20;

The British introduced restrictions on Jewish immigration to Palestine with the [White Paper of 1939](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Paper_of_1939). With countries around the world turning away Jewish refugees fleeing [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust), a clandestine movement known as [Aliyah Bet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliyah_Bet) was organised to bring Jews to Palestine. During [World War II](32927.md), Palestine was [repeatedly bombed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_bombing_of_Mandatory_Palestine_in_World_War_II) by Axis aircraft, causing casualties among both Jews and Arabs. About 30,000 Jews from Palestine served in the British military during the war, of whom around 700 were killed.[^cite_note-181] Some 12,000 Palestinian Arabs also served in the British military during the war.[^cite_note-182] At the end of the war, about 31% of the population of Palestine was Jewish.[^cite_note-183] The UK found itself facing a [Jewish insurgency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_insurgency_in_Mandatory_Palestine) over immigration restrictions from 1944, which intensified following the end of the war, and continued conflict with the Arab community over limit levels. The Haganah joined Irgun and Lehi in an armed struggle against British rule.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFraser200427-184] The Haganah attempted to bring tens of thousands of Jewish refugees and [Holocaust survivors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_survivors) to Palestine by ship. Most of the ships were intercepted by the [Royal Navy](26061.md) and the refugees placed in detention camps in [Atlit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlit_detainee_camp) and [Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus_internment_camps).[^cite_note-Golani2013-185][^cite_note-186] The insurgency included bombings and other attacks by Jewish insurgent organisations, including attacks that had killed civilians as well as British security personnel and officials.&#x20;

On 22 July 1946, Irgun [bombed the British administrative headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_David_Hotel_bombing) for Palestine in the southern wing of King David Hotel, killing 91.[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-brtca_irgun-188][^cite_note-tclarke81-189][^cite_note-bethell-190] The attack was intended to destroy documents that Irgun believed implicated the Jewish Agency in attacks against the British during [Operation Agatha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Agatha), a series of raids, including one on the [Jewish Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Agency_for_Israel). It was the deadliest attack against the British during the Mandate era.[^cite_note-tclarke81-189][^cite_note-bethell-190] The Jewish insurgency continued throughout 1946 and 1947 despite concerted efforts by the British military and [Palestine Police Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Police_Force) to suppress it.&#x20;

British efforts to mediate with Jewish and Arab representatives were not successful as the Jews were unwilling to accept any solution that did not involve a Jewish state and suggested a partition of Palestine into Jewish and Arab states, while the Arabs were adamant that a Jewish state in any part of Palestine was unacceptable and that the only solution was a unified Palestine under Arab rule. In February 1947, the British referred the Palestine issue to the newly formed [United Nations](31769.md). On 15 May 1947, the [UN General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Assembly_of_the_United_Nations) resolved that a [Special Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Committee_on_Palestine) be created "to prepare ... a report on the question of Palestine".[^cite_note-191] The Report of the Committee[^cite_note-192] [proposed a plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine) to replace the British Mandate with "an independent Arab State, an independent Jewish State, and the City of Jerusalem \[...] the last to be under an International Trusteeship System".[^cite_note-193]&#x20;

Meanwhile, the Jewish insurgency continued and peaked in July 1947, with a series of widespread guerrilla raids culminating in [the Sergeants affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sergeants_affair), in which the Irgun took two British sergeants hostage as attempted leverage against the planned execution of three Irgun operatives. After the executions were carried out, the Irgun killed the two British soldiers, hanged their bodies from trees, and left a booby trap at the scene which injured a British soldier. The incident caused widespread outrage in the UK.[^cite_note-Hoffman-194] In September 1947, the British cabinet decided to evacuate Palestine as the Mandate was no longer tenable.[^cite_note-195]&#x20;

### 1948 war and establishment of the State of Israel

Further information: [1948 Palestine war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestine_war) and [Arab–Israeli conflict](7960202.md)



[1947 partition plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine) of Palestine between Israel and the [State of Palestine](241405.md), as proposed by the [United Nations](31769.md)

On 29 November 1947, the General Assembly adopted [Resolution 181 (II)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Partition_Plan_for_Palestine), which recommended the partition of Mandatory Palestine into separate Jewish and Arab states with Jerusalem being under an international regime.[^cite_note-181(II)-196] The plan attached to the resolution was essentially that proposed in the report of 3 September and had allocated the larger share of territory (55–56%) to The Jewish Agency, the recognised representative of the Jewish community, despite the Jewish population were about a third of the population, most of whom were recent immigrants, and owned around 6–7% of the land. At the time, the Arabs constituted the majority population and owned about 20% of the land, with the remainder held by the Mandate authorities or foreign landowners. The Jewish leadership accepted the plan but the Arab leadership rejected it.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-Morris2008396-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200]&#x20;

The [Arab League](52625.md) and [Arab Higher Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Higher_Committee) of Palestine responded that the partition plan privileged European interests over those of the Palestinians,[^cite_note-201] and indicated that they would "refuse to be bound" by any resolution to divide the country. They argued that the resolution was contrary to the principle of [self-determination](29269.md) and that Arabs constituted the majority of its "lawful inhabitants", who had consistently expressed their desire for their country to "remain united and undivided".[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-\:0-36][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman200240–41-203] On 1 December 1947, the Arab Higher Committee proclaimed a three-day strike, and [riots broke out in Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947_Jerusalem_riots).[^cite_note-204] The situation spiralled into [a civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947%E2%80%931948_civil_war_in_Mandatory_Palestine). Colonial Secretary [Arthur Creech Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Creech_Jones) announced that the British Mandate would end on 15 May 1948, at which point the British would evacuate. As Arab militias and gangs attacked Jewish areas, they were faced mainly by the Haganah as well as the smaller Irgun and Lehi. In April 1948, the Haganah moved onto the offensive, a phase of fighting that included the [Deir Yassin massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Yassin_massacre), in which Irgun and Lehi forces attacked the Arab village of [Deir Yassin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deir_Yassin) on 9 April, killing more than 100 villagers according to some estimates, including women and children.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris200877–78-205][^cite_note-206]&#x20;



[David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion) declaring the [establishment of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence) on 14 May 1948

On 14 May 1948, the day before the expiration of the British Mandate, [David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion), the head of the Jewish Agency, [declared "the establishment of a Jewish state in Eretz-Israel"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Declaration_of_Independence).[^cite_note-207] The following day, the armies of four Arab countries—Egypt, Syria, Transjordan, and Iraq—entered what had been Mandatory Palestine, launching the [1948 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBen-Sasson19851058-208] contingents from [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutawakkilite_Kingdom_of_Yemen), [Morocco](19291.md), [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), and [Sudan](27421.md) joined the war.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris2008205-209][^cite_note-210] The purpose of the invasion was to prevent the establishment of the Jewish state.[^cite_note-Morris2008396-198][^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] The Arab League stated the invasion was to restore order and prevent further bloodshed.[^cite_note-cablegram-213]&#x20;



Raising of the [Ink Flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ink_Flag) on 10 March 1949, marking the end of the [1948 war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War)

After a year of fighting, a [ceasefire was declared](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949_Armistice_Agreements) and temporary borders, known as the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)), were established.[^cite_note-214] [Jordan annexed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_annexation_of_the_West_Bank) what became known as the [West Bank](33209.md), including [East Jerusalem](343508.md), and [Egypt occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_the_Gaza_Strip_by_the_United_Arab_Republic) the [Gaza Strip](12047.md). Over 700,000 Palestinians [fled or were expelled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestinian_expulsion_and_flight) by [Zionist militias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_political_violence) and the [Israeli military](39237.md)—what would become known in Arabic as the *[Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba)* ('catastrophe').[^cite_note-215] The events also led to the destruction of most of Palestine's Arab [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Palestine), [identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_identity), and [national aspirations](47246228.md). Some 156,000 Arabs remained and became [Arab citizens of Israel](492331.md).[^cite_note-\:2-216]&#x20;

By [United Nations General Assembly Resolution 273](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_273), Israel was admitted as a member of the UN on 11 May 1949.[^cite_note-217] In the early years of the state, the [Labour Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Zionism) movement led by Prime Minister Ben-Gurion dominated [Israeli politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Israel).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELustick198837–39-218][^cite_note-219] Immigration to Israel during the late 1940s and early 1950s was aided by the Israeli Immigration Department and the non-government sponsored [Mossad LeAliyah Bet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mossad_LeAliyah_Bet) (lit. "Institute for [Immigration B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliyah_Bet)").[^cite_note-220] The latter engaged in clandestine operations in countries, particularly in the Middle East and Eastern Europe, where the lives of Jews were in danger and exit was difficult. Mossad LeAliyah Bet was disbanded in 1953.[^cite_note-221] The immigration was in accordance with the [One Million Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Million_Plan). Some immigrants held Zionist beliefs or came for the promise of a better life, while others moved to escape persecution or were expelled from their homes.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223]&#x20;

An [influx of Holocaust survivors](351513.md#Early_statehood_\(1948%E2%80%931960\)) and [Jews from Arab and Muslim countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_exodus_from_the_Muslim_world) to Israel during the first three years increased the number of Jews from 700,000 to 1,400,000. By 1958, the population had risen to two million.[^cite_note-224] Between 1948 and 1970, approximately 1,150,000 Jewish refugees relocated to Israel.[^cite_note-225] Some immigrants arrived as refugees and were housed in temporary camps known as *[ma'abarot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma%27abarot)*; by 1952, over 200,000 people were living in these tent cities.[^cite_note-226] [Jews of European background](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jews) were often treated more favourably than Jews from [Middle Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizrahi_Jews) and [North African](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardi_Jews) countries—housing units reserved for the latter were often re-designated for the former, so Jews newly arrived from Arab lands generally ended up staying longer in transit camps.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegev2007155–157-228] During this period, food, clothes and furniture were rationed in what became known as the [austerity period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austerity_in_Israel). The need to solve the crisis led Ben-Gurion to sign a [reparations agreement with West Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reparations_Agreement_between_Israel_and_the_Federal_Republic_of_Germany) that triggered mass protests by Jews angered at the idea that Israel could accept monetary compensation for the Holocaust.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShindler200249–50-229]&#x20;

There were [further expulsions of Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949%E2%80%931956_Palestinian_expulsions) after the establishment of Israel. During the 1950s, Israel was [frequently attacked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_attacks_against_Israeli_civilians_before_1967) by [Palestinian fedayeen](14068131.md), nearly always against civilians,[^cite_note-230] mainly from the Egyptian-occupied Gaza Strip,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGilbert200558-231] leading to several Israeli [reprisal operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reprisal_operations). In 1956, the UK and France aimed at regaining control of the [Suez Canal](29323.md), which Egypt had nationalised. The continued blockade of the Suez Canal and [Straits of Tiran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Straits_of_Tiran) to Israeli shipping, together with increasing fedayeen attacks against Israel's southern population and recent Arab threatening statements, prompted Israel to attack Egypt.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] Israel joined [a secret alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protocol_of_S%C3%A8vres) with the UK and France and overran the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula) in the [Suez Crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suez_Crisis) but was pressured to withdraw by the UN in return for guarantees of Israeli shipping rights.[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237] The war resulted in significant reduction of Israeli border infiltration.[^cite_note-238]&#x20;

U.S. newsreel on the trial of [Adolf Eichmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Eichmann)

In the early 1960s, Israel captured Nazi war criminal [Adolf Eichmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Eichmann) in Argentina and brought him to Israel [for trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eichmann_trial).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBascomb2009219–229-239][^cite_note-240] Eichmann remains the only person executed in Israel by conviction in an Israeli civilian court.[^cite_note-241] In 1963, Israel was engaged in a diplomatic standoff with the United States in relation to the Israeli [nuclear programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_Israel).[^cite_note-Haaretz2019-242][^cite_note-243]&#x20;

Since 1964 Arab countries, concerned over Israeli plans to divert waters of the [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River) into the [coastal plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_coastal_plain),[^cite_note-244] had been trying to divert the headwaters to deprive Israel of water resources, [provoking tensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_over_Water_\(Jordan_River\)) between Israel on the one hand, and Syria and Lebanon on the other. [Arab nationalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_nationalist) led by Egyptian President [Gamal Abdel Nasser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamal_Abdel_Nasser) refused to recognise Israel and called for its destruction.[^cite_note-RoutledgeAtlas-245][^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] By 1966, Israeli-Arab relations had deteriorated to the point of battles taking place between Israeli and Arab forces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegev2007178-248]&#x20;

In May 1967, Egypt massed its army near the border with Israel, expelled [UN peacekeepers stationed in the Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Emergency_Force) since 1957, and blocked Israel's access to the Red Sea.[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] Other Arab states mobilised their forces.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegev2007289-252] Israel reiterated that these actions were a *[casus belli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casus_belli)* and launched a pre-emptive strike ([Operation Focus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Focus)) against Egypt in June. Jordan, Syria and Iraq attacked Israel.&#x20;



Territory held by Israel:&#x20;

&#x20; before the [Six-Day War](29328.md)

&#x20; after the war

&#x20;The [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula) was returned to Egypt in 1982.

In the ensuing [Six-Day War](29328.md), Israel captured and occupied the West Bank from Jordan, the Gaza Strip and Sinai Peninsula from Egypt, and the Golan Heights from Syria.[^cite_note-253] Israeli forces [expelled \~300,000 Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naksa) from the Gaza Strip and the West Bank. Jerusalem's boundaries were enlarged, incorporating [East Jerusalem](343508.md). The 1949 Green Line became the administrative boundary between Israel and the [occupied territories](2781576.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShlayRosen2010362–363-254]&#x20;

Following the 1967 war and the "[Three Nos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khartoum_Resolution)" resolution of the Arab League, Israel faced attacks from the Egyptians in the Sinai Peninsula during the 1967–1970 [War of Attrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_Attrition), and from Palestinian groups targeting Israelis in the occupied territories, globally, and in Israel. Most important among the Palestinian and Arab groups was the [Palestine Liberation Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organisation) (PLO), established in 1964, which initially committed itself to "armed struggle as the only way to liberate the homeland".[^cite_note-255] In the late 1960s and early 1970s, [Palestinian groups launched attacks](2646043.md)[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] against Israeli and Jewish targets around the world,[^cite_note-258] including [a massacre of Israeli athletes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Munich_massacre) at the [1972 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics) in Munich. The Israeli government responded with an [assassination campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mossad_assassinations_following_the_Munich_massacre) against the organisers of the massacre, [a bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Israeli_air_raid_in_Syria_and_Lebanon) and a [raid on the PLO headquarters in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1973_Israeli_raid_in_Lebanon).&#x20;

On 6 October 1973, the Egyptian and Syrian armies launched [a surprise attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Badr_\(1973\)) against Israeli forces in the Sinai Peninsula and Golan Heights, opening the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War). The war ended on 25 October with Israel repelling Egyptian and Syrian forces but suffering great losses.[^cite_note-259] An [internal inquiry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agranat_Commission) exonerated [the government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifteenth_government_of_Israel) of responsibility for failures before and during the war, but public anger forced Prime Minister [Golda Meir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golda_Meir) to resign.[^cite_note-260] On 27 June 1976, [Air France Flight 139](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_France_Flight_139) was hijacked in flight from Israel to France by Palestinian guerrillas; Israeli commandos rescued 102 of 106 Israeli hostages days later.[^cite_note-261]&#x20;

The [1977 Knesset elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1977_Israeli_legislative_election) marked a significant turning point in Israeli political history as [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin)'s [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud) party took control from the [Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party).[^cite_note-262] Later that year, Egyptian President [Anwar El Sadat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_El_Sadat) made a trip to Israel and spoke before the [Knesset](74656.md) in what was the first recognition of Israel by an Arab head of state.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002171–174-263] Sadat and Begin signed the [Camp David Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David_Accords) (1978) and the [Egypt–Israel peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty) (1979).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002186–187-264] In return, Israel withdrew from the Sinai Peninsula and agreed to enter negotiations over autonomy for Palestinians in the West Bank and the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002186–187-264]&#x20;

On 11 March 1978, a PLO guerilla raid from Lebanon led to the [Coastal Road massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_Road_massacre). Israel responded by launching an [invasion of southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_South_Lebanon_conflict) to destroy PLO bases. Begin's government meanwhile provided incentives for [Israelis to settle](15123.md) in the [occupied West Bank](59076253.md), increasing friction with the Palestinians there.[^cite_note-265]&#x20;

The 1980 [Jerusalem Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_Law) was believed by some to reaffirm Israel's 1967 annexation of Jerusalem by government decree and [reignited international controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Security_Council_Resolution_478) over the [status of the city](4024423.md). No Israeli legislation has defined the territory of Israel, and no act specifically included East Jerusalem therein.[^cite_note-266] In 1981 Israel [effectively annexed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golan_Heights_Law) the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-bbc_golan_profile-267] The international community largely rejected these moves, with the UN Security Council declaring both the Jerusalem Law and the Golan Heights Law null and void.[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269] Several waves of [Ethiopian Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beta_Israel) [immigrated to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliyah_from_Ethiopia) since the 1980s, while between 1990 and 1994, [immigration from the post-Soviet states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990s_post-Soviet_aliyah) increased Israel's population by twelve per cent.[^cite_note-270]&#x20;

On 7 June 1981, during the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War), the [Israeli air force destroyed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Opera) Iraq's sole nuclear reactor, then under construction, in order to impede the Iraqi nuclear weapons programme.[^cite_note-271] Following a series of PLO attacks in 1982, Israel [invaded Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War) to destroy the PLO bases.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002199-272] In the first six days, Israel destroyed the military forces of the PLO in Lebanon and decisively defeated the Syrians. An Israeli government inquiry (the [Kahan Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kahan_Commission)) held Begin and several Israeli generals indirectly responsible for the [Sabra and Shatila massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabra_and_Shatila_massacre) and held [defence minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Israel\)) [Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon) as bearing "personal responsibility".[^cite_note-273] Sharon was forced to resign.[^cite_note-274]&#x20;

In 1985, Israel responded to a Palestinian [terrorist attack in Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larnaca_yacht_killings) by [bombing the PLO headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Wooden_Leg) in Tunisia. Israel withdrew from most of Lebanon in 1986 but continued to [occupy a borderland buffer zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)) in southern Lebanon until 2000, from where Israeli forces [engaged in conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_conflict_\(1985%E2%80%932000\)) with [Hezbollah](13919.md). The [First Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Intifada), a Palestinian uprising against Israeli rule,[^cite_note-275] broke out in 1987, with waves of uncoordinated demonstrations and violence in the occupied West Bank and Gaza. Over the following six years, the intifada became more organised and included economic and cultural measures aimed at disrupting the Israeli occupation. Over 1,000 people were killed.[^cite_note-276] During the 1991 [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), the PLO supported [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein) and Iraqi missile [attacks against Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_rocket_attacks_on_Israel). Despite public outrage, Israel heeded American calls to refrain from hitting back.[^cite_note-277][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMowlanaGerbnerSchiller1992111-278]&#x20;



[Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) (left) with [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin) (center) and King [Hussein of Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_of_Jordan) (right), prior to signing the [Israel–Jordan peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_peace_treaty) in 1994

In 1992, [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin) became prime minister following [an election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Israeli_legislative_election) in which his party called for compromise with Israel's neighbours.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002236-279][^cite_note-280] The following year, [Shimon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres) on behalf of Israel and [Yasser Arafat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasser_Arafat) for the PLO signed the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), which gave the [Palestinian National Authority](24093.md) (PNA) the right to govern [parts of the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank_areas_in_the_Oslo_II_Accord) and the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-281] The PLO also [recognised Israel's right to exist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_Liberation_Organization_letters_of_recognition) and pledged an end to terrorism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETessler2009729-282] In 1994, the [Israel–Jordan peace treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Jordan_peace_treaty) was signed, making Jordan the second Arab country to normalise relations with Israel.[^cite_note-283] Arab public support for the Accords was damaged by the continuation of Israeli settlements[^cite_note-284] and [checkpoints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_checkpoint), and the deterioration of economic conditions.[^cite_note-285] Israeli public support for the Accords waned after [Palestinian suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_suicide_attacks).[^cite_note-286] In November 1995, Rabin [was assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Yitzhak_Rabin) by [Yigal Amir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yigal_Amir), a far-right Jew who opposed the Accords.[^cite_note-287]&#x20;

During [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md)'s premiership at the end of the 1990s, Israel [agreed to withdraw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protocol_Concerning_the_Redeployment_in_Hebron) from [Hebron](38577.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002257-288] though this was never ratified or implemented,[^cite_note-289] and he signed the [Wye River Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wye_River_Memorandum). The agreement dealt with further redeployments in the West Bank and security issues. The memorandum was criticised by major international human rights organisations for its "encouragement" of human rights abuses.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291] [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak), [elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Israeli_general_election) prime minister in 1999, withdrew forces from southern Lebanon and conducted negotiations with PNA Chairman Yasser Arafat and U.S. President [Bill Clinton](3356.md) at the [2000 Camp David Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Camp_David_Summit). Barak offered a plan for the establishment of a [Palestinian state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_state), including the entirety of the Gaza Strip and over 90% of the West Bank with Jerusalem as a shared capital.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGelvin2005240-292] Each side blamed the other for the failure of the talks.&#x20;

### 21st century

Further information: [Iran–Israel proxy conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict), [Gaza–Israel conflict](16346266.md), and [Middle Eastern crisis (2023–present)](75895273.md)

In late 2000, after a controversial visit by Sharon to the [Temple Mount](65719.md), the [Second Intifada](71717.md) began. The popular uprising faced disproportionate repression from the Israeli state.[^cite_note-293] [Palestinian suicide bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_suicide_attacks) eventually developed into a recurrent feature of the intifada.[^cite_note-294] Some commentators contend that the intifada was pre-planned by Arafat after the collapse of peace talks.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296][^cite_note-jewishweek-297][^cite_note-atlantic-298] Sharon became prime minister in a [2001 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2001_Israeli_prime_ministerial_election); he carried out his plan to [unilaterally withdraw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_the_Gaza_Strip) from the Gaza Strip and spearheaded the construction of the [West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank_barrier),[^cite_note-299] ending the intifada.[^cite_note-300] Between 2000 and 2008, 1,063 Israelis, 5,517 Palestinians and 64 foreign citizens were killed.[^cite_note-301]&#x20;

In July 2006, a Hezbollah artillery assault on Israel's northern border communities and a [cross-border abduction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Hezbollah_cross-border_raid) of two Israeli soldiers precipitated the month-long [Second Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), including an Israeli invasion of Lebanon.[^cite_note-UN1701-302][^cite_note-HRTZ_Harel-303] The war wound down in August 2006 after the passage of [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1701](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1701); Israeli forces mostly withdrew from Lebanon by October 2006 but continued to occupy the Lebanese portion of [Ghajar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghajar) village.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] In 2007 the Israeli Air Force [destroyed a nuclear reactor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Outside_the_Box) in Syria.&#x20;

In 2008, [a ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_ceasefire) between [Hamas](13913.md) and Israel collapsed, resulting in the three-week [Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%932009\)).[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-ravid-307] In what Israel described as a response to [over a hundred Palestinian rocket attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel_in_2012) on southern Israeli cities,[^cite_note-pound-308] Israel began [an operation in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Gaza_War) in 2012, lasting eight days.[^cite_note-309] Israel started another [operation in Gaza](43243199.md) following an escalation of rocket attacks by Hamas in July 2014.[^cite_note-310] In May 2021, another [round of fighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_crisis) took place in Gaza and Israel, lasting eleven days.[^cite_note-311] By the 2010s, [increasing regional cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_alliance) between Israel and [Arab League](52625.md) countries have been established, culminating in the signing of the [Abraham Accords](64883396.md). The Israeli security situation shifted from the traditional [Arab–Israeli conflict](7960202.md) towards the [Iran–Israel proxy conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict) and [direct confrontation with Iran during the Syrian civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_conflict_during_the_Syrian_civil_war).&#x20;



Satellite view of fires in the [Gaza envelope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_envelope) on 7 October 2023

On 7 October 2023, Palestinian militant groups from [Gaza](12047.md), led by Hamas, launched [a series of coordinated attacks](74998317.md) on Israel, leading to the start of the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-312] On that day, approximately 1,300 Israelis, predominantly civilians, were killed in communities near the Gaza Strip border and [during a music festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re%27im_music_festival_massacre). [Over 200 hostages](75025451.md) were kidnapped and taken to the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-Gillett-313][^cite_note-314][^cite_note-natureparty-315] Studies modeling trauma exposure and assessing mental health outcomes estimated that approximately 5.3% of Israelis may develop [PTSD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PTSD),[^cite_note-316] with national data showing that probable PTSD nearly doubled from 16.2% to 29.8% and rates of anxiety and depression also rising sharply.[^cite_note-317]&#x20;

After clearing militants from its territory, Israel launched [one of the most destructive bombing campaigns in modern history](76139149.md)[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319] and [invaded Gaza](74998519.md) on 27 October with the stated objectives of destroying Hamas and freeing hostages.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] The fifth war of the [Gaza–Israel conflict](16346266.md) since 2008, it has been the deadliest for Palestinians in the entire [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md)[^cite_note-322] and the most significant military engagement in the region since the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War) in 1973.[^cite_note-El_Deeb-2023-323] A [United Nations Special Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Committee_to_Investigate_Israeli_Practices_Affecting_the_Human_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People), [multiple governments](75736183.md), and various experts and human rights organisations have concluded that Israel is committing [genocide against the Palestinian people](75668427.md) due to the harm and loss of life inflicted on civilians during the [Gaza War](74998519.md).[^cite_note-324][^cite_note-ohchr-325][^cite_note-326]&#x20;

In April 2024, Israel initiated a wave of airstrikes on Iran, after Iranian strikes targeted Israel, marking the [2024 Iran–Israel conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Iran%E2%80%93Israel_conflict) the first time in which the two countries have ever directly exchanged fire. In October 2024, Israel [invaded Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israeli_invasion_of_Lebanon)[^cite_note-327] and exchanged missile barrages with Iran three weeks later, in response of Iranian strikes earlier in that month.[^cite_note-328] After nearly a year of the [Israel–Hezbollah conflict](75005425.md) from October 2023 due to Hezbollah shooting rockets at Israel to support Hamas in Gaza, Israel [assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike) Hezbollah secretary general [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) in September 2024.[^cite_note-329] A November 2024 [ceasefire agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement) instructed Israel to withdraw from Lebanon, which Israel mostly did by February 2025, but against the agreement, Israeli forces stayed in five military outposts on highlands in Southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] In June 2025, Israel launched a renewed series of airstrikes on Iran, targeting Iran's air defence systems, missile launchers, their military leadership, and their [nuclear programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_programme_of_Iran), which escalated into a [full-scale war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War).[^cite_note-332] In February 2026, Israel and the United States launched extensive airstrikes on Iran, [assassinating](82537558.md) Iranian Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) and sparking the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) and the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md). The strikes aimed to destroy the Iranian military, nuclear programme, and [achieve regime change](82756412.md).[^cite_note-333]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Israel)
