[Satellite images](1184557.md) of Israel and neighbouring territories during the day and night

Israel is located in the [Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant) area of the [Fertile Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertile_Crescent). At the [eastern end](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Mediterranean) of the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md), it is bounded by Lebanon to the north, Syria to the north-east, Jordan and the West Bank to the east, and Egypt and the Gaza Strip to the south-west. It lies between latitudes [29°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/29th_parallel_north) and [34° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/34th_parallel_north), and longitudes [34°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/34th_meridian_east) and [36° E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/36th_meridian_east).&#x20;

The sovereign territory of Israel (according to the demarcation lines of the [1949 Armistice Agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1949_Armistice_Agreements) and excluding all territories captured by Israel during the 1967 Six-Day War) is approximately 20,770 square kilometres (8,019 sq mi), of which two per cent is water.[^cite_note-cia-19] However Israel is so narrow (100 km at its widest, compared to 400 km from north to south) that the [exclusive economic zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone) in the Mediterranean is double the land area of the country.[^cite_note-334] The total area under Israeli law, including East Jerusalem and the Golan Heights, is 22,072 square kilometres (8,522 sq mi),[^cite_note-335] and the total area under Israeli control, including the military-controlled and partially Palestinian-governed territory of the West Bank, is 27,799 square kilometres (10,733 sq mi).[^cite_note-loc-geo-336]&#x20;

Despite its small size, Israel is home to a variety of geographic features, from the [Negev](142273.md) desert in the south to the inland fertile [Jezreel Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jezreel_Valley), with mountain ranges of the [Galilee](12639.md), [Carmel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Carmel) and towards the [Golan](7515865.md) in the north. The [Israeli coastal plain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_coastal_plain) on the shores of the Mediterranean is home to most of the population.[^cite_note-337] East of the central highlands lies the [Jordan Rift Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Rift_Valley), a small part of the 6,500-kilometre (4,039 mi) [Great Rift Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Rift_Valley). The [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River) runs along the Jordan Rift Valley, from [Mount Hermon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Hermon) through the [Hulah Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hulah_Valley) and the [Sea of Galilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_of_Galilee) to the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea), the [lowest point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extreme_points_of_Earth) on the surface of the Earth.[^cite_note-338] Further south is the [Arabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabah), ending with the [Gulf of Eilat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Aqaba), part of the [Red Sea](26191.md). [Makhtesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makhtesh), or "erosion cirques" are unique to the Negev and the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula), the largest being the [Makhtesh Ramon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makhtesh_Ramon) at 38 km in length.[^cite_note-339] Israel has the largest number of plant species per square metre of the countries in the Mediterranean Basin[^cite_note-rinat-340] and contains four terrestrial ecoregions: [Eastern Mediterranean conifer–sclerophyllous–broadleaf forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Mediterranean_conifer%E2%80%93sclerophyllous%E2%80%93broadleaf_forests), [Southern Anatolian montane conifer and deciduous forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Anatolian_montane_conifer_and_deciduous_forests), [Arabian Desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Desert), and [Mesopotamian shrub desert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamian_shrub_desert).[^cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-341] Forests accounted for 8.5% of the area in 2016, up from 2% in 1948, as the result of a large-scale forest planting programme by the [Jewish National Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_National_Fund).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343]&#x20;

### Tectonics and seismicity

Further information: [List of earthquakes in the Levant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_earthquakes_in_the_Levant)

The Jordan Rift Valley is the result of tectonic movements within the [Dead Sea Transform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea_Transform) (DST) fault system. The DST forms the [transform boundary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transform_fault) between the [African Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Plate) to the west and the [Arabian Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Plate) to the east. The Golan Heights and all of Jordan are part of the Arabian Plate, while the Galilee, West Bank, Coastal Plain, and Negev along with the Sinai Peninsula are on the African Plate. This tectonic disposition leads to a relatively high seismic activity. The entire Jordan Valley segment is thought to have ruptured repeatedly, for instance during the last two major earthquakes along this structure in [749](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/749_Galilee_earthquake) and [1033](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1033_Jordan_Valley_earthquake). The deficit in [slip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fault_\(geology\)#Slip,_heave,_throw) that has built up since 1033 is sufficient to cause an earthquake of M<sub>w</sub>\~7.4.[^cite_note-Ferry-344]&#x20;

The most catastrophic known earthquakes occurred in 31 BCE, [363](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/363_Galilee_earthquake), 749, and 1033 CE, that is every ca. 400 years on average.[^cite_note-MarcoAFTAU-345] Destructive earthquakes strike about every 80 years, leading to serious loss of life .[^cite_note-IllPreped-346] While stringent construction regulations are in place and recently built structures are earthquake resistant, as of 2007 many public buildings as well as 50,000 residential buildings did not meet the new standards and were "expected to collapse" if exposed to a strong earthquake.[^cite_note-IllPreped-346]&#x20;

### Climate

Further information: [Climate change in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Israel)



The projections of the [IPCC Sixth Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPCC_Sixth_Assessment_Report) show clearly the impacts of climate change on Israel even at 2 degrees of warming.

Temperatures vary widely, especially during the winter. Coastal areas, such as those of Tel Aviv and Haifa, have a typical [Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate) with cool, rainy winters and long, hot summers. The area of Beersheba and the northern Negev have a [semi-arid climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-arid_climate) with hot summers, cool winters, and fewer rainy days. The southern Negev and the Arabah areas have a [desert climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desert_climate) with very hot, dry summers, and mild winters with few days of rain. The highest temperature of 54 °C (129 °F) was recorded in 1942 in the [Tirat Zvi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirat_Zvi) kibbutz.[^cite_note-watzman-347][^cite_note-348] Mountainous regions can be windy and cold, and areas at elevation of 750 metres (2,460 ft) or more (same elevation as Jerusalem) usually receive at least one [snowfall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snow_in_Israel) each year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldreich200385-349] From May to September, rain is rare.[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351]&#x20;

There are four different [phytogeographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phytogeography) regions, due to its location between the temperate and tropical zones. For this reason, the flora and fauna are extremely diverse. There are 2,867 known [species of plants in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_endemic_flora_of_Israel). Of these, at least 253 species are [introduced and non-native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_adventive_wild_plants_in_Israel).[^cite_note-352] There are 380 [Israeli nature reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks_and_nature_reserves_of_Israel).[^cite_note-353]&#x20;

With scarce water resources, Israel has developed various water-saving technologies, including [drip irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drip_irrigation).[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355] The considerable sunlight available for [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_energy) makes [Israel the leading nation in solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Israel) use per capita—practically every house uses solar panels for water heating.[^cite_note-Solar_energy-356] The [Ministry of Environmental Protection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Environmental_Protection_\(Israel\)) has reported that [climate change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_change_in_Israel) "will have a decisive impact on all areas of life", particularly for vulnerable populations.[^cite_note-MEP_2020-357]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Israeli system of government](74576.md) and [Politics of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Israel)



[Isaac Herzog](4645588.md)\
[President](153049.md)&#x20;



[Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md)\
[Prime Minister](24898.md)&#x20;



The [Knesset](74656.md) chamber, home to the Israeli parliament

Israel has a [parliamentary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system), [proportional representation](23298.md) and [universal suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_suffrage). A member of parliament supported by a parliamentary majority becomes the [prime minister](24898.md)—usually this is the chair of the largest party. The prime minister is the [head of government](37570.md) and of [cabinet](5006115.md).[^cite_note-cia2-358][^cite_note-359] The [president](153049.md) is [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state), with largely ceremonial duties.[^cite_note-cia2-358]&#x20;

Israel is governed by a 120-member parliament, known as the [Knesset](74656.md). Membership of the Knesset is based on proportional representation of [political parties](240166.md),[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361] with a 3.25% electoral threshold, which in practice has resulted in coalition governments. Residents of Israeli settlements in the West Bank are eligible to vote,[^cite_note-362] and after the [2015 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Israeli_legislative_election), 10 of the 120 members of the Knesset (8%) were settlers.[^cite_note-363] Parliamentary [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Israel) are scheduled every four years, but unstable coalitions or a [no-confidence vote](208101.md) can dissolve a government earlier.[^cite_note-cnn-51] The first Arab-led party was established in 1988,[^cite_note-364] and as of 2022, Arab-led parties hold about 10% of seats.[^cite_note-365] A party cannot run for election to the Knesset if its objectives or actions include the "negation of the existence of the State of Israel as the state of the Jewish people".&#x20;

The [Basic Laws of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Laws_of_Israel) function as an [uncodified constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uncodified_constitution). These define Israel as a [Jewish and democratic state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_and_democratic_state) and the nation-state of exclusively the Jewish people.[^cite_note-freedomhouse2008-366] In 2003, the Knesset began to draft an official constitution based on these laws.[^cite_note-cia-19][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMazie200634-367]&#x20;

Israel has no official religion,[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] but the definition of the state as "Jewish and democratic" creates a strong connection with [Judaism](15624.md). On 19 July 2018, the Knesset passed a Basic Law that characterises Israel as principally a "[Nation State of the Jewish People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Law:_Israel_as_the_Nation-State_of_the_Jewish_People)" and Hebrew as its official language. The bill ascribes an undefined "special status" to the Arabic language.[^cite_note-371] The same bill gives Jews a unique right to national self-determination and views the developing of Jewish settlement in the country as "a national interest", empowering the government to "take steps to encourage, advance and implement this interest".[^cite_note-372]&#x20;

The State of Israel is divided into six main administrative districts, known as *mehozot* (Hebrew: מחוזות; sing.: *mahoz*)—[Center](844282.md), [Haifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haifa_District), [Jerusalem](844333.md), [North](324281.md), [South](844338.md), and [Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_District), as well as the [Judea and Samaria Area](397650.md) in the West Bank. All of the Judea and Samaria Area and parts of the Jerusalem and Northern districts are not recognised internationally as part of Israel. Districts are divided into 15 sub-districts known as *nafot* (Hebrew: נפות; sing.: *nafa*), which are partitioned into 50 natural regions.[^cite_note-373]&#x20;

### Israeli citizenship law

Main article: [Israeli citizenship law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_citizenship_law)

The 1950 [Law of Return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Return) grants Jews the unrestricted [right to immigrate](54035.md#Restricted_jus_soli) to Israel and obtain Israeli citizenship. Individuals born within the country receive [birthright citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jus_sanguinis) if at least one parent is a citizen.[^cite_note-376] Israeli law defines Jewish nationality as distinct from Israeli nationality, and the [Supreme Court of Israel](1666510.md) has ruled that an Israeli nationality does not exist.[^cite_note-tekiner-377][^cite_note-378] A Jewish national is defined as any person practicing Judaism and their descendants.[^cite_note-tekiner-377]&#x20;

| [Gaza Strip](12047.md) | [Palestinian National Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority) (*de jure*) Controlled by [Hamas](13913.md) (*de facto*) | Witnesses to the [Oslo II Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_II_Accord) | [Palestine](241405.md) | [157 UN member states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_Palestine) |
| [West Bank](33209.md) | [Palestinian enclaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_enclaves) (Areas A and B) | [Palestinian National Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority) and [Israeli military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories) |  |  |
| [Area C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_C) | [Israeli enclave law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_law_in_the_West_Bank_settlements) ([Israeli settlements](15123.md)) and Israeli military (Palestinians under [Israeli occupation](59076253.md)) |  |  |  |
| [East Jerusalem](343508.md) | [Israeli administration](74576.md) | [Honduras](13394.md), [Guatemala](17238567.md), [Nauru](21302.md), and the [United States](3434750.md) | [China](5405.md), [Russia](25391.md) |  |
| [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem) | [Russia](25391.md), [Czech Republic](5321.md), Honduras, Guatemala, Nauru, and the United States | [United Nations](31769.md) as an [international city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_separatum_\(Jerusalem\)) along with East Jerusalem | Various UN member states and the [European Union](9317.md); [joint sovereignty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Condominium_\(international_law\)) also widely supported |  |
| [Golan Heights](7515865.md) | United States and [Colombia](5222.md) | [Syria](7515849.md) | All UN member states except the United States and Colombia |  |
| [Israel (Green Line border)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)) | [165 UN member states](32359767.md) | [Israel](9282173.md) | 165 UN member states |  |

\
&#x20;



Map of Israel showing the West Bank, the Gaza Strip, and the Golan Heights

In 1967, as a result of the Six-Day War, Israel captured and occupied the West Bank (including East Jerusalem) from Jordan, the Gaza Strip and the Sinai Peninsula from Egypt, and the Golan Heights from Syria. After an agreement was reached between Israel and Syria a year after the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War), a [buffer zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNDOF_Zone) monitored by the [United Nations Disengagement Observer Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Disengagement_Observer_Force) (UNDOF) was established in 1974.[^cite_note-379] Also sparked by the Yom Kippur War, Egypt and Israel reached a peace agreement due to the [1978 Camp David Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_David_Accords) and the [1979 Egypt-Israel Peace Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_Egypt-Israel_Peace_Treaty), both mediated by former American President [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBregman2002186–187-264] In the mediation, Egypt agreed to formally recognise Israel and to heavily demilitarise the Sinai,[^cite_note-380] in exchange for Israel to fully return the Sinai to Egypt in 1982;[^cite_note-381] however, Egypt refused to take in the Gaza Strip and it therefore remained under Israeli occupation until the [2005 disengagement plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] Between 1982 and 2000, Israel occupied [part of southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)), in what was known as the [Security Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_security_belt_administration). Since capture of these territories, Israeli settlements and military installations have been built within each of them, except Lebanon.&#x20;

The Golan Heights and East Jerusalem have been fully incorporated under Israeli law but not under international law. Israel has applied civilian law to both areas and granted their inhabitants permanent residency status and the ability to apply for citizenship. The UN Security Council has declared the annexation of the Golan Heights and East Jerusalem to be "null and void" and continues to view the territories as occupied.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] The status of East Jerusalem in any future peace settlement has at times been a difficult issue in negotiations between Israeli governments and representatives of the Palestinians.&#x20;



The [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier) is a separation barrier built by Israel along the Green Line and inside parts of the West Bank.

The West Bank area, excluding East Jerusalem, is known as [the Judea and Samaria Area.](397650.md)The almost 400,000 Israeli settlers residing in the area are considered part of Israel's population, have Knesset representation, are subject to a [large part of Israel's civil and criminal laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave_law), and their output is considered part of Israel's economy.[^cite_note-Sher-386][^cite_note-oecd-25] The land is not considered part of Israel under Israeli law, as Israel has consciously refrained from annexing the territory, without ever relinquishing its legal claim to the land or defining a border.[^cite_note-Sher-386] Israeli political opposition to annexation primarily stems from the perceived "demographic threat" of incorporating the West Bank's Palestinian population into Israel.[^cite_note-Sher-386] Outside of the Israeli settlements, the West Bank remains under direct Israeli military rule, and Palestinians in the area cannot become Israeli citizens.&#x20;

The international community maintains that Israel does not have sovereignty in the West Bank and considers Israel's control of the area to be the longest military occupation in modern history.[^cite_note-occhist-389] The West Bank was occupied and annexed by Jordan in 1950, following the 1949 Armistice Agreements. Only Britain recognised this annexation, and Jordan has since [ceded its claim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_disengagement_from_the_West_Bank) to the territory to the PLO. The population is mainly Palestinians, including refugees of the 1948 Arab–Israeli War.[^cite_note-390] From their occupation in 1967 until 1993, the Palestinians living in these territories were under [Israeli military administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories). Since the [Israel–PLO letters of recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_Liberation_Organization_letters_of_recognition), most of the Palestinian population and [cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_administered_by_the_State_of_Palestine) have been under the internal jurisdiction of the Palestinian Authority, and only partial Israeli military control, although Israel has redeployed its troops and reinstated full military administration during periods of unrest. Israel's claim of universal suffrage has been questioned due to its blurred territorial boundaries, its simultaneous extension of voting rights to Israeli settlers in the occupied territories and denial of voting rights to their Palestinian neighbours, as well as the alleged [ethnocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnocracy) nature of the state.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392]&#x20;

The Gaza Strip is considered to be a "foreign territory" under Israeli law. Israel and Egypt operate a land, air, and sea [blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md). The Gaza Strip was occupied by Israel after 1967. In 2005, as part of a [unilateral disengagement plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%27s_unilateral_disengagement_plan), Israel removed its settlers and forces from the territory but continues to maintain control of its airspace and waters. The international community, including numerous international humanitarian organisations and UN bodies, consider Gaza to remain occupied.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] Following the 2007 Battle of Gaza, when [Hamas assumed power in the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas_government_in_the_Gaza_Strip),[^cite_note-gaza-398] Israel tightened control of the Gaza crossings along [its border](478337.md), as well as by sea and air, and prevented persons from entering and exiting except for isolated cases it deemed humanitarian.[^cite_note-gaza-398] Gaza has a [border with Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Gaza_border), and an agreement between Israel, the EU, and the PA governs how border crossings take place.[^cite_note-399] The application of democracy to its Palestinian citizens and the selective application of Israeli democracy in the Israeli-controlled Palestinian territories have been criticised.[^cite_note-Slater2020-400][^cite_note-White2012-401]&#x20;

#### International opinion

See also: [Israeli war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes)

The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) said, in its [2004 advisory opinion](1368578.md#2004_advisory_opinion_of_the_ICJ) on the legality of the construction of the West Bank barrier, that the lands captured by Israel in the Six-Day War, including East Jerusalem, are occupied territory and found that the construction of the wall within the occupied Palestinian territory violates international law.[^cite_note-402] Most negotiations relating to the territories have been on the basis of [UN Security Council Resolution 242](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_242), which emphasises "the inadmissibility of the acquisition of territory by war", and calls on Israel to withdraw from occupied territories in return for normalisation of relations with Arab states ("[Land for peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_for_peace)").[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405] Israel has been criticised for engaging in systematic and widespread violations of [human rights in the occupied territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_the_Israeli-occupied_territories), including occupation[^cite_note-406] and war crimes against civilians.[^cite_note-tny1-407][^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] The allegations include violations of international humanitarian law[^cite_note-411] by the [UN Human Rights Council](635790.md).[^cite_note-412] The [U.S. State Department](31975.md) has called reports of [abuses of significant human rights of Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_violations_against_Palestinians_by_Israel) "credible" both within Israel[^cite_note-413] and the occupied territories.[^cite_note-414] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and other NGOs have documented mass arbitrary arrests, torture, unlawful killings, systemic abuses and impunity[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416][^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418] in tandem with a denial of the right to [Palestinian self-determination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_self-determination).[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420][^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] Prime Minister Netanyahu has defended the country's security forces for protecting the innocent from terrorists[^cite_note-424] and expressed contempt for what he describes as a lack of concern about the human rights violations committed by "criminal killers".[^cite_note-425]&#x20;

The [international community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_community) widely regards Israeli settlements in the occupied territories [illegal under international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements).[^cite_note-426] [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2334](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334) (passed 2016) states that Israel's settlement activity constitutes a "flagrant violation" of [international law](8195726.md) and demands that Israel stop such activity and fulfill its obligations as an [occupying power](816214.md#The_occupying_power) under the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention).[^cite_note-toi-427] A [United Nations special rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur) concluded that the settlement programme was a war crime under the [Rome Statute](68762.md),[^cite_note-428] and [Amnesty International](18947898.md) found that the settlement programme constitutes an illegal transfer of civilians into occupied territory and "pillage", which is prohibited by the [Hague Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hague_Conventions_of_1899_and_1907) and [Geneva Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Conventions) as well as being a war crime under the Rome Statute.[^cite_note-429]&#x20;

In a [2024 advisory opinion](1368578.md#2024_advisory_opinion_of_the_ICJ), the International Court of Justice stated that occupation of the Palestinian territories violated international law; Israel should end its occupation as quickly as possible and pay reparations. In addition, the court found that Israel was in breach of article 3 of the [International Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Racial Discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Convention_on_the_Elimination_of_All_Forms_of_Racial_Discrimination), which requires states to prevent, prohibit and eradicate all practices of racial segregation and apartheid.[^cite_note-430][^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432]&#x20;

#### Accusations of apartheid

Main article: [Israeli apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_apartheid)

Treatment of Palestinians within the occupied territories and to a lesser extent in Israel itself have drawn widespread accusations that it is guilty of [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_of_apartheid), a [crime against humanity](61099.md) under the Rome Statute and the [International Convention on the Suppression and Punishment of the Crime of Apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Convention_on_the_Suppression_and_Punishment_of_the_Crime_of_Apartheid).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShakir2021-433][^cite_note-ATC-434] *The Washington Post'*&#x73; 2021 survey of scholars and academic experts on the Middle East found an increase from 59% to 65% of these scholars describing Israel as a "one-state reality akin to apartheid".[^cite_note-sf-45][^cite_note-\:9-46] The claim that Israel's policies for Palestinians [within Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_citizens_of_Israel) or [within Israeli-occupied territories](2781576.md) amount to apartheid has been affirmed by Israeli human rights organisation [B'tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%27tselem),[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem2021-436] University Network for Human Rights,[^cite_note-437] and international human rights organisations such as Amnesty International[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2022-438] and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md).[^cite_note-ATC-434][^cite_note-Arno-439] Israeli human rights organisation [Yesh Din](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesh_Din) has also accused Israel of apartheid.[^cite_note-Arno-439] Amnesty's claim was criticised by politicians and representatives from Israel and its closest allies such as, the US,[^cite_note-440] the UK,[^cite_note-441] the [European Commission](9974.md),[^cite_note-442] Australia,[^cite_note-443] Netherlands[^cite_note-444] and Germany,[^cite_note-445] while said accusations were welcomed by Palestinians[^cite_note-446] and the [Arab League](52625.md).[^cite_note-447] In 2022, Michael Lynk, a Canadian law professor [appointed by the U.N. Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Special_Rapporteur_on_the_occupied_Palestinian_territories) said that the situation met the legal definition of apartheid, and concluded: "Israel has imposed upon Palestine an apartheid reality in a post-apartheid world".[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] Subsequent reports from his successor, [Francesca Albanese](72566699.md) and from [Permanent United Nations Fact Finding Mission on the Israel Palestine conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_United_Nations_Fact_Finding_Mission_on_the_Israel_Palestine_conflict) chair Navi Pillay echoed the opinion.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451]&#x20;

In February 2024, the [ICJ held public hearings](72240449.md) in regards to the legal consequences arising from the policies and practices of Israel in the occupied Palestinian territory including East Jerusalem. During the hearings, 24 states and three international organisations said that Israeli practices amount to a breach of the prohibition of apartheid and/or amount to prohibited acts of racial discrimination.[^cite_note-452] The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) in its [2024 advisory opinion](72240449.md) found that Israel's occupation of the Palestinian territories constitutes systemic discrimination and is in breach of Article 3 of the [International Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Racial Discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Convention_on_the_Elimination_of_All_Forms_of_Racial_Discrimination), which prohibits racial segregation and apartheid. The opinion is silent as to whether the discrimination amounts to apartheid; individual judges were split on the question.[^cite_note-auto7-453][^cite_note-454][^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-auto7-453][^cite_note-auto8-457]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main articles: [Foreign relations of Israel](74581.md) and [International recognition of Israel](32359767.md)



&#x20; Israel

&#x20; States that recognize Israel

&#x20; States that recognize Israel, but have suspended relations

&#x20; States that do not recognize Israeli statehood

Israel maintains diplomatic relations with 165 [UN member states](31969.md), the [Holy See](13393.md), [Kosovo](17391.md), the [Cook Islands](7067.md) and [Niue](21228.md). It has 107 [diplomatic missions](6838552.md);[^cite_note-458] countries with which it has no diplomatic relations include most Muslim countries.[^cite_note-459] Six out of 22 nations in the [Arab League](52625.md) have normalised relations with Israel. Israel remains formally in a [state of war with Syria](23533046.md), dating back uninterrupted to 1948. It has been in a similarly [formal state of war with Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_relations) since the end of the [Lebanese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Civil_War) in 2000, with the Israel–Lebanon border remaining unagreed by treaty.&#x20;

Despite the peace treaty between Israel and Egypt, Israel is still widely considered an enemy country among Egyptians.[^cite_note-460] Iran withdrew its recognition of Israel during the [Islamic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Revolution).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbadi200447–49-461] Israeli citizens may not visit Syria, Lebanon, Iraq, Saudi Arabia, and Yemen without permission from the [Ministry of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_\(Israel\)).[^cite_note-462] As a result of the 2008–09 Gaza War, Mauritania, Qatar, Bolivia, and Venezuela suspended political and economic ties with Israel,[^cite_note-al-jaz-eng-463] though Bolivia renewed ties in 2019.[^cite_note-464]&#x20;



Yitzhak Rabin and Yasser Arafat at the signing ceremony of the [Oslo Accords peace agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), with then US President [Bill Clinton](3356.md) (center)

The [United States](3449549.md) and the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Russia_relations) were the first two countries to recognise the State of Israel, having declared recognition roughly simultaneously.[^cite_note-465] Diplomatic relations with the Soviet Union were broken in 1967 following the Six-Day War and renewed in 1991.[^cite_note-466] The United States regards Israel as its "most reliable partner in the Middle East",[^cite_note-467] based on "common democratic values, religious affinities, and security interests".[^cite_note-468] The US has provided \$68 billion [in military assistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations) and \$32 billion in grants to Israel since 1967, under the [Foreign Assistance Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Assistance_Act) (period beginning 1962),[^cite_note-PNADR900-469] more than any other country for that period until 2003.[^cite_note-PNADR900-469][^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471] Most surveyed Americans have held consistently favourable views of Israel.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473] The United Kingdom is seen as having a "natural" [relationship](20968669.md) with Israel because of the Mandate for Palestine.[^cite_note-474] By 2007, [Germany](6545438.md) had paid 25 billion euros in [reparations to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reparations_Agreement_between_Israel_and_West_Germany) and individual Israeli Holocaust survivors.[^cite_note-475] Israel [is included](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93European_Union_relations) in the European Union's [European Neighbourhood Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Neighbourhood_Policy).[^cite_note-476]&#x20;

Although [Turkey and Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) did not establish full diplomatic relations until 1991,[^cite_note-477] Turkey has cooperated with the Jewish state since its recognition of Israel in 1949. Turkey's ties to other Muslim-majority nations in the region have at times resulted in pressure from Arab and Muslim states to temper its relationship with Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbadi20044–6-478] Relations took a downturn after the 2008–09 Gaza War and Israel's [raid of the Gaza flotilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid).[^cite_note-479] Relations between [Greece and Israel](16119434.md) have improved since 1995 after decline of Israeli–Turkish relations.[^cite_note-480] The two countries have a defence cooperation agreement and in 2010, the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) hosted Greece's [Hellenic Air Force](363254.md) in a joint exercise. The joint Cyprus-Israel oil and gas explorations centred on the [Leviathan gas field](30257584.md) are an important factor for Greece, given its [strong links](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Greece_relations) with Cyprus.[^cite_note-481] Cooperation in the world's longest [submarine power cable](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Submarine_power_cable), the [EuroAsia Interconnector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EuroAsia_Interconnector), has strengthened [Cyprus–Israel relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Israel_relations).[^cite_note-482]&#x20;

Azerbaijan is one of the few majority Muslim countries to develop strategic and economic [relations with Israel](11716339.md).[^cite_note-adalah-483] Kazakhstan also has an economic and strategic partnership with Israel.[^cite_note-484] India established full [diplomatic ties](7199609.md) with Israel in 1992 and has fostered a strong military, technological and cultural partnership with the country since then.[^cite_note-485] India is the largest customer of the [Israeli military equipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_military_equipment), and Israel is the second-largest military partner of India after Russia.[^cite_note-486] [Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopia%E2%80%93Israel_relations) is Israel's main ally in Africa due to common political, religious and security interests.[^cite_note-487]&#x20;

As of 2025, Israel is the only UN member state to recognise the [Republic of Somaliland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Somaliland).[^cite_note-Somaliland-488][^cite_note-DaV6b-489][^cite_note-T3L29-490]&#x20;

#### Foreign aid

Israel has a history of providing emergency foreign aid and humanitarian response to disasters across the world.[^cite_note-491] In 1955 Israel began its [foreign aid programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_foreign_aid) in Burma and then shifted to Africa.[^cite_note-Geldenhuys-492] Israel's humanitarian efforts officially began in 1957 with the establishment of [Mashav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mashav), the Israel's Agency for International Development Cooperation.[^cite_note-493] In this early period, whilst Israel's aid represented only a small percentage of total aid to Africa, its programme was effective in creating goodwill; however, following the 1967 war relations soured.[^cite_note-Ismael-494] Israel's foreign aid programme subsequently shifted its focus to Latin America.[^cite_note-Geldenhuys-492]&#x20;

Since the late 1970s Israel's foreign aid has gradually decreased, although in recent years Israel has tried to reestablish aid to Africa.[^cite_note-495] There are additional Israeli humanitarian and emergency response groups that work with the government, including [IsraAid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IsraAid), a joint programme run by Israeli organisations and North American Jewish groups,[^cite_note-496] [ZAKA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZAKA),[^cite_note-497] The Fast Israeli Rescue and Search Team,[^cite_note-498] Israeli Flying Aid,[^cite_note-499] [Save a Child's Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Save_a_Child%27s_Heart)[^cite_note-500] and [Latet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latet).[^cite_note-501] Between 1985 and 2015, Israel sent 24 delegations of their search and rescue unit the [Home Front Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_Front_Command) to 22 countries.[^cite_note-502] Currently Israeli foreign aid [ranks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_development_aid_country_donors) low among [OECD](33853117.md) nations, spending less than 0.1% of its [GNI](1242956.md) on development assistance.[^cite_note-503] The country ranked 38th in the 2018 [World Giving Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Giving_Index).[^cite_note-504]&#x20;

### Military

Further information: [List of wars involving Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wars_involving_Israel), [List of the Israel Defense Forces operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_Israel_Defense_Forces_operations), and [Israel and weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction)



[F-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) fighter jets of the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md)

The [Israel Defence Forces](39237.md) (IDF) is the sole military wing of the [Israeli security forces](315904.md) and is headed by its [Chief of the General Staff](3011266.md), the *Ramatkal*, subordinate to the Cabinet. The IDF consists of the [army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GOC_Army_Headquarters), [air force](177619.md) and [navy](1370788.md). It was founded during the 1948 Arab–Israeli War by consolidating paramilitary organisations, chiefly the Haganah.[^cite_note-505] The IDF also draws upon the resources of the [Military Intelligence Directorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Intelligence_Directorate_\(Israel\)) (*Aman*).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHenderson200397-506]&#x20;

Most [Israelis are conscripted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_Israel) at age 18. Men serve two years and eight months, and [women serve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Israel_Defence_Forces) two years.[^cite_note-507] Following mandatory service, Israeli men join the reserve forces and usually do up to several weeks of [reserve duty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_duty_\(Israel\)) every year until their forties. Most women are exempt from reserve duty. [Arab citizens of Israel](492331.md) (except the [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_in_Israel)) and those engaged in full-time religious studies [are exempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exemption_from_military_service_in_Israel), although the [exemption of yeshiva students](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tal_committee) has been a source of contention.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStendel1997191–192-508][^cite_note-509] An alternative for those who receive exemptions on various grounds is *[Sherut Leumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherut_Leumi)*, or national service, which involves a programme of service in social welfare frameworks.[^cite_note-510] A small minority of Israeli Arabs also volunteer in the army.[^cite_note-511] As a result of its conscription programme, the IDF maintains approximately 176,500 active troops and 465,000 reservists, giving Israel one of the world's highest [percentage of citizens with military training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_military_and_paramilitary_personnel).[^cite_note-IISS_military-512]&#x20;



[Iron Dome](18342427.md) is the world's first operational anti-[artillery rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_artillery) defence system.

The military relies heavily on high-tech [weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_equipment_of_Israel) systems [designed and manufactured in Israel](39305449.md) as well as some foreign imports. The [Arrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrow_\(Israeli_missile\)) missile is one of the world's few operational [anti-ballistic missile](1791.md) systems.[^cite_note-513] The [Python](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_\(missile\)) air-to-air missile series is often considered one of the most crucial weapons in its military history.[^cite_note-514] Israel's [Spike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spike_\(missile\)) missile is one of the most widely exported [anti-tank guided missiles](37517.md) in the world.[^cite_note-515] Israel's [Iron Dome](18342427.md) anti-missile air defence system gained worldwide acclaim after intercepting hundreds of [rockets fired by Palestinian militants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel) from the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-Johnson-516][^cite_note-Tory-517] Since the [Yom Kippur War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yom_Kippur_War), Israel has developed a network of [reconnaissance satellites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconnaissance_satellites).[^cite_note-518] The *[Ofeq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofeq)* programme has made Israel [one of seven countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_first_orbital_launches_by_country) capable of launching such satellites.[^cite_note-519]&#x20;

Israel is widely believed to [possess nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_Israel)[^cite_note-520] and per a 1993 report, chemical and biological [weapons of mass destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction).[^cite_note-521] Israel has not signed the [Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons](22107.md)[^cite_note-522] and maintains a [policy of deliberate ambiguity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Policy_of_deliberate_ambiguity) towards its nuclear capabilities.[^cite_note-523] The Israeli Navy's [Dolphin submarines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolphin-class_submarine) are believed to be armed with nuclear missiles offering [second-strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_strike) capability.[^cite_note-524] Since the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) in 1991, all homes in Israel are required to have a reinforced security room, [Merkhav Mugan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkhav_Mugan), impermeable to chemical and biological substances.[^cite_note-525]&#x20;

Since Israel's establishment, military expenditure constituted a significant portion of the country's [gross domestic product](12594.md), with peak of 30.3% of GDP in 1975.[^cite_note-526] In 2021, Israel ranked 15th in the world [by total military expenditure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_military_expenditures), with \$24.3 billion, and 6th by defence spending as a percentage of GDP, with 5.2%.[^cite_note-527] Since 1974, the United States has been a particularly notable contributor of [military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations#Military_aid_and_procurement).[^cite_note-528] Under a [memorandum of understanding](1018279.md) signed in 2016, the U.S. is expected to provide the country with \$3.8 billion per year, or around 20% of Israel's defence budget, from 2018 to 2028.[^cite_note-529] Israel ranked 8th globally for [arms exports](608296.md) in 2020–2024.[^cite_note-530] The majority of Israel's arms exports are unreported for security reasons.[^cite_note-531] Israel is consistently rated low in the [Global Peace Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Peace_Index), ranking 134th out of 163 nations in 2022.[^cite_note-GPI-532]&#x20;

### Legal system

Main articles: [Judiciary of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_Israel) and [Israeli law](8932627.md)



[Supreme Court of Israel](1666510.md), Givat Ram, Jerusalem

Israel has a [three-tier court system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_judicial_system). At the lowest level are [magistrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magistrate) courts, situated in most cities across the country. Above them are [district courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_court), serving as both [appellate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appeal) courts and [courts of first instance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trial_court); they are situated in five of Israel's six [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Israel). The third and highest tier is the [Supreme Court](1666510.md), located in Jerusalem; it serves a dual role as the highest court of appeals and the [High Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Court_of_Justice_\(Israel\)). In the latter role, the Supreme Court rules as a court of first instance, allowing both citizens and non-citizens to petition against the decisions of state authorities.[^cite_note-533]&#x20;

The legal system combines three legal traditions: [English common law](85441.md), [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)), and [Jewish law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halakha).[^cite_note-cia-19] It is based on the principle of *[stare decisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stare_decisis)* (precedent) and is an [adversarial system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adversarial_system). Court cases are decided by professional judges.[^cite_note-judiciary-534] [Marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marriage_in_Israel) and divorce are under the jurisdiction of the religious courts: [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beth_din), [Muslim](28840.md), Druze, and Christian. The election of judges is carried out by a [selection committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judicial_Selection_Committee_\(Israel\)) chaired by the [justice minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Justice_\(Israel\)) (currently [Yariv Levin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yariv_Levin)).[^cite_note-535] Israel's [Basic Law: Human Dignity and Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Law:_Human_Dignity_and_Liberty) seeks to defend [human rights and liberties in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Israel). The [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md) and Israeli human rights organisation [Adalah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adalah_\(legal_center\)) have highlighted that this law does not in fact contain a general provision for equality and non-discrimination.[^cite_note-adalah-483][^cite_note-536] As a result of "[Enclave law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave_law)", large portions of Israeli civil law are applied to Israeli settlements and Israeli residents in the occupied territories.[^cite_note-Ben-NaftaliSfard2018-537] Israeli law is seldom enforced in the occupied territories, with 96% of police investigations into settler violence in the West Bank from 2020 to 2025 ending without an indictment. No one has been charged in the killings of at least 1,100 Palestinian civilians by Israeli soldiers and settlers since 2020.[^cite_note-538]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Israel](14690.md)



The [Diamond Exchange District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond_Exchange_District) in [Ramat Gan](312761.md)

Israel is considered the most advanced country in [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Asia) and the Middle East in economic and industrial development.[^cite_note-Chua_2003_219–220-539][^cite_note-540] As of October 2023, the IMF estimated its GDP at 521.7 billion dollars and GDP per capita at 53.2 thousand ([ranking 13th worldwide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)_per_capita)).[^cite_note-541] It is the third richest country in Asia [by nominal per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)_per_capita) income[^cite_note-542] and has the highest average [wealth per adult](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_wealth_per_adult) in the Middle East.[^cite_note-CS_2019-1-543] *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* ranked Israel as the fourth most successful economy among the developed countries for 2022.[^cite_note-544] It has the [most billionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_billionaires) in the Middle East and the 18th most in the world.[^cite_note-545] In recent years, Israel had one of the highest growth rates in the developed world.[^cite_note-546] In 2010, it joined the [OECD](33853117.md).[^cite_note-OECD-547][^cite_note-548] The country is ranked 35th on the [World Bank](36752.md)'s [*Ease of Doing Business* index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ease_of_doing_business_index).[^cite_note-549] Economic data covers the economic territory of Israel, including the Golan Heights, East Jerusalem and Israeli settlements in the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOECD2011-387]&#x20;

Despite limited natural resources, intensive development of the [agricultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Israel) and industrial sectors over the past decades has made Israel largely self-sufficient in food production, apart from grains and beef. Imports, totalling \$96.5 billion in 2020, include raw materials, military equipment, investment goods, rough diamonds, fuels, grain, and consumer goods.[^cite_note-cia-19] Leading exports include machinery, equipment, software, [cut diamonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diamond_industry_in_Israel), agricultural products, chemicals, textiles, and apparel; in 2020, exports reached \$114 billion.[^cite_note-cia-19] The [Bank of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Israel) holds \$201 billion of foreign-exchange reserves, the 17th highest in the world.[^cite_note-cia-19] Since the 1970s, Israel has received [military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93United_States_military_relations) from the United States, as well as loan guarantees, which account for roughly half of Israel's external debt. Israel has [one of the lowest](3001143.md) external debts in the developed world, and is a lender in terms of net external debt ([assets vs. liabilities abroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_international_investment_position)), which in 2015 stood at a surplus of \$69 billion.[^cite_note-550]&#x20;

Israel has the second-largest number of startup companies after the United States[^cite_note-551] and the third-largest number of [NASDAQ-listed companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_companies_quoted_on_the_Nasdaq).[^cite_note-552] It is the world leader for number of start-ups per capita[^cite_note-553] and has been dubbed the "[Start-Up Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Start-up_Nation)".[^cite_note-554][^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556][^cite_note-557] [Intel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel)[^cite_note-558] and [Microsoft](19001.md)[^cite_note-559] built their first overseas [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) facilities in Israel, and other high-tech multinational corporations have opened [research and development centres in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_multinational_companies_with_research_and_development_centres_in_Israel).&#x20;

The days which are allocated to working times are Sunday through Thursday (for a five-day workweek), or Friday (for a six-day workweek). In observance of [Shabbat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabbat), in places where Friday is a work day and the majority of population is Jewish, Friday is a "short day". Several proposals have been raised to adjust the work week with the majority of the world.[^cite_note-560]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main articles: [Science and technology in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Israel) and [List of Israeli inventions and discoveries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_inventions_and_discoveries)



[Matam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matam,_Haifa) high-tech park in Haifa

Israel's development of cutting-edge technologies in software, communications and the life sciences have [evoked comparisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Wadi) with [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley).[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562] Israel is first in the world in [expenditure on research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_research_and_development_spending) as a percentage of GDP.[^cite_note-OECD_R\&D-563] It is ranked 14th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025,[^cite_note-564][^cite_note-565] and 5th in the 2019 [Bloomberg Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_Innovation_Index).[^cite_note-Bloomberg_innovation-566] Israel has 140 scientists, technicians, and engineers per 10,000 employees, the highest number in the world[^cite_note-567][^cite_note-568] and has produced six [Nobel Prize-winning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_Nobel_laureates) scientists, mostly in chemistry, since 2004[^cite_note-nobel-569] and has been frequently ranked as one of the countries with the highest ratios of [scientific papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_papers) per capita.[^cite_note-570][^cite_note-571][^cite_note-572] [Israeli universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_universities_and_colleges) are ranked among the top 50 world universities in computer science ([Technion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technion) and [Tel Aviv University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_University)), mathematics ([Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem)) and chemistry ([Weizmann Institute of Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weizmann_Institute_of_Science)).[^cite_note-ARWU-573]&#x20;

In 2012, Israel was ranked ninth in the world by the Futron's [Space Competitiveness Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Competitiveness_Index).[^cite_note-574] The [Israel Space Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Space_Agency) coordinates all space research programmes with scientific and commercial goals, and have designed and built at least 13 commercial, research and spy satellites.[^cite_note-575] Some satellites are ranked among the world's most advanced space systems.[^cite_note-576] [Shavit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shavit_2) is a space [launch vehicle](1306157.md) produced by Israel to launch small satellites into [low Earth orbit](47568.md).[^cite_note-577] It was first launched in 1988, making Israel the [eighth nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_first_orbital_launches_by_country) to have a space launch capability. In 2003, [Ilan Ramon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ilan_Ramon) became Israel's first astronaut, serving on the [fatal mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle_Columbia_disaster) of [Space Shuttle *Columbia*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_Shuttle_Columbia).[^cite_note-578]&#x20;

The [ongoing water shortage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Israel) has spurred innovation in water conservation techniques, and a substantial [agricultural modernisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_research_in_Israel), [drip irrigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drip_irrigation), was invented in Israel. Israel is also at the technological forefront of desalination and water recycling. The Sorek desalination plant is the largest seawater [reverse osmosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reverse_osmosis) desalination facility in the world.[^cite_note-579] By 2014, desalination programmes provided roughly 35% of the drinking water, and it is expected to supply 70% by 2050.[^cite_note-580] As of 2015, over 50 per cent of the water for households, agriculture and industry is artificially produced.[^cite_note-Kershner-581] In 2011, Israel's water technology industry was worth around \$2 billion per year with annual exports of products and services in the tens of millions of dollars. As a result of innovations in reverse osmosis technology, Israel is set to become a net [exporter of water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_export).[^cite_note-582]&#x20;

A horizontal parabolic dish, with a triangular structure on its top.

The world's largest [solar parabolic dish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parabolic_reflector) at the [Ben-Gurion National Solar Energy Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben-Gurion_National_Solar_Energy_Center)

[^cite_note-Register-583]

Israel has embraced [solar energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Israel); its engineers are on the cutting edge of solar energy technology,[^cite_note-NPR-584] and its solar companies work on projects around the world.[^cite_note-CBC-585][^cite_note-BW-586] Over 90% of homes use solar energy for hot water, the highest per capita.[^cite_note-Solar_energy-356][^cite_note-Environment_California_SWH-587] According to government figures, the country saves 8% of its electricity consumption per year because of its solar energy use in heating.[^cite_note-588] The high annual incident solar irradiance at its geographic latitude creates ideal conditions for what is an internationally renowned solar research and development industry in the Negev.[^cite_note-NPR-584][^cite_note-CBC-585][^cite_note-BW-586] Israel had a modern electric car infrastructure involving a countrywide network of charging stations;[^cite_note-589][^cite_note-590][^cite_note-591] however, its electric car company [Better Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Better_Place_\(company\)) shut down in 2013.[^cite_note-592]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Israel)

Israel [began producing natural gas](51593574.md) from its own offshore gas fields in 2004. In 2009 [Tamar gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamar_gas_field) was discovered near the coast, and [Leviathan gas field](30257584.md) was discovered in 2010.[^cite_note-593] The natural gas reserves in these two fields could make Israel energy-secure for more than 50 years. Commercial production of natural gas from the Tamar field began in 2013, with over 7.5 billion cubic metres (bcm) produced annually.[^cite_note-594] Israel had 199 billion bcm of proven reserves of natural gas as of 2016.[^cite_note-595] The Leviathan gas field started production in 2019.[^cite_note-596]&#x20;

[Ketura Sun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ketura_Sun) is Israel's first commercial solar field. Built in 2011 by the [Arava Power Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arava_Power_Company), the field will produce about 9 gigawatt-hours (32 TJ) of electricity per year,[^cite_note-597] sparing the production of some 125,000 tonnes (123,000 long tons; 138,000 short tons) of carbon dioxide over 20 years.[^cite_note-598]&#x20;

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Israel)



[Ben Gurion International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Gurion_International_Airport), the main international airport of Israel

As of 2016, Israel has 19,224 kilometres (11,945 mi) of paved [roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_in_Israel)[^cite_note-599] and 3 million motor vehicles.[^cite_note-vehicles-600] The [number of motor vehicles per 1,000 persons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_vehicles_per_capita) is 365, relatively low among developed countries.[^cite_note-vehicles-600] The country aims to have 30% of vehicles on its roads powered by electricity by 2030.[^cite_note-601]&#x20;

Israel has 5,715 buses on scheduled routes,[^cite_note-602] operated by several carriers, the largest and oldest of which is [Egged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egged_\(company\)), serving most of the country.[^cite_note-603] [Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Israel) stretch across 1,277 kilometres (793 mi) and are operated by government-owned [Israel Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Railways).[^cite_note-cbs_rails-604] Following major investments beginning in the early to mid-1990s, the number of train passengers per year has grown from 2.5 million in 1990, to 53 million in 2015; railways transport 7.5 million tonnes (8.3 million short tons; 7.4 million long tons) of cargo per year.[^cite_note-cbs_rails-604]&#x20;

Israel is served by three international [airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Israel): [Ben Gurion Airport](113149.md), the country's main hub for international air travel; [Ramon Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramon_Airport); and [Haifa Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haifa_Airport). Ben Gurion handled over 21.1 million passengers in 2023.[^cite_note-605] There are three main ports: the [Port of Haifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Haifa), the oldest and largest; [Ashdod Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Ashdod); and the [Port of Eilat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Eilat) on the [Red Sea](26191.md).&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Israel)

See also: [List of archaeological sites in Israel and Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_archaeological_sites_in_Israel_and_Palestine)



[Ein Bokek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ein_Bokek) resort on the shore of the [Dead Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dead_Sea)

Tourism, especially [religious tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_tourism), is an important industry, with [beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_beaches_in_Israel), [archaeological](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeology_of_Israel), other [historical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Israel) and [biblical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_biblical_places) sites, and unique geography also drawing tourists. In 2017, a record 3.6 million tourists visited Israel, yielding a 25 per cent growth since 2016 and contributed NIS 20 billion to the economy.[^cite_note-606][^cite_note-607]&#x20;

### Real estate

Main article: [Housing in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_Israel)

Housing prices are listed in the top third of all countries,[^cite_note-608] with an average of 150 salaries required to buy an apartment.[^cite_note-609] As of 2022, there are about 2.7 million properties in Israel, with an annual increase of over 50,000.[^cite_note-610] However, demand for housing exceeds supply, with a shortage of about 200,000 apartments as of 2021.[^cite_note-611] As a result, by 2021 housing prices rose by 5.6%.[^cite_note-612] In 2021, Israelis took a record of NIS 116.1 billion in mortgages, an increase of 50% from 2020.[^cite_note-613]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Israel) and [Israelis](181296.md)



[Aliyah](351513.md) in the years 1948–2015. The two peaks were in 1949 and 1990.

Israel has the largest Jewish population in the world and is the only country where Jews are the majority.[^cite_note-614] As of April 2025, the population was an estimated 10,094,000.[^cite_note-615] In 2025, the government recorded 72% of the population as [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Jews), 21% as [Arabs](492331.md), and 7% as "Others" (non-Arab Christians and people who have no religion listed).[^cite_note-population_stat2024-17] Over the last decade, large numbers of migrant workers from Romania, Thailand, China, Africa, and South America have settled in Israel. Exact figures are unknown, as many of them are living in the country illegally,[^cite_note-616] but estimates run from 166,000 to 203,000.[^cite_note-Adriana_Kemp-617] By June 2012, approximately 60,000 [African migrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_immigration_from_Africa_to_Israel) had entered Israel.[^cite_note-618]&#x20;

About 93% of Israelis live in urban areas.[^cite_note-619] 90% of Palestinian Israelis reside in 139 densely populated towns and villages concentrated in the Galilee, [Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triangle_\(Israel\)) and Negev regions, with the remaining 10% in [mixed cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_cities) and neighbourhoods.[^cite_note-620] The OECD in 2016 estimated the average life expectancy at 82.5 years, the [6th-highest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy).[^cite_note-OECD_life_expec-621] Israeli Arab life expectancy lags by 3 to 4 years[^cite_note-622][^cite_note-623] and is higher than in most Arab and Muslim countries.[^cite_note-624][^cite_note-625] The country has the highest [fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) in the OECD and the only one which is above the replacement figure of 2.1.[^cite_note-\:5-626] Retention of Israel's population since 1948 is about even or greater, when compared to other countries with mass immigration.[^cite_note-627] Jewish emigration from Israel (called *[yerida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yerida)*), primarily to the United States and Canada, is described by demographers as modest,[^cite_note-628] but is often cited by Israeli government ministries as a major threat to Israel's future.[^cite_note-629][^cite_note-630]&#x20;

Approximately 80% of [Israeli Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Jews) are [born in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabra_\(person\)), 14% are immigrants from Europe and the Americas, and 6% are immigrants from Asia and Africa.[^cite_note-631] Jews from Europe and the former Soviet Union and their descendants born in Israel, including Ashkenazi Jews, constitute approximately 44% of Jewish Israelis. Jews from Arab and Muslim countries and their descendants, including both Mizrahi and Sephardi Jews,[^cite_note-632] form most of the rest of the Jewish population.[^cite_note-633] Jewish intermarriage rates run at over 35% and recent studies suggest that the percentage of Israelis descended from both Sephardi and Ashkenazi Jews increases by 0.5 per cent yearly, with over 25% of schoolchildren now originating from both.[^cite_note-634] Around 4% of Israelis (300,000), ethnically defined as "others", are Russian descendants of Jewish origin or family who are not Jewish according to rabbinical law, but were eligible for citizenship under the Law of Return.[^cite_note-635][^cite_note-Israel_people-636][^cite_note-637]&#x20;

Israeli settlers beyond the Green Line number over 600,000 (≈10% of the Jewish Israeli population).[^cite_note-638] In 2016, [399,300 Israelis lived](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_statistics_for_Israeli_settlements_in_the_West_Bank) in West Bank settlements,[^cite_note-districts_pop-374] including those that predated the establishment of the State of Israel and which were re-established after the Six-Day War. Additionally there were more than 200,000 Jews living in East Jerusalem[^cite_note-jerusalem_pop-375] and 22,000 in the Golan Heights.[^cite_note-districts_pop-374] Approximately 7,800 Israelis [lived in settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_statistics_for_Israeli_Gaza_Strip_settlements) in the Gaza Strip, known as [Gush Katif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Katif), until they were evacuated by the government as part of its 2005 [disengagement plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%27s_unilateral_disengagement_plan).[^cite_note-639]&#x20;

Israeli Arabs (including the Arab population of East Jerusalem and the Golan Heights) comprise 21.1% of the population or 1,995,000 people.[^cite_note-640] In a 2017 poll, 40% of Arab citizens of Israel identified as "Arab in Israel" or "Arab citizen of Israel", 15% identified as "Palestinian", 8.9% as "Palestinian in Israel" or "Palestinian citizen of Israel", and 8.7% as "Arab"; the same poll found that 60% of Israeli Arabs have a positive view of the state.[^cite_note-641][^cite_note-642]&#x20;

### Major urban areas

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of cities in Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Israel).



View over the [Tel Aviv Metropolitan Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_Metropolitan_Area)



[Israel cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Israel)

Israel has four major metropolitan areas: [Gush Dan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Dan) (Tel Aviv metropolitan area; population 3,854,000), [Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerusalem_metropolitan_area) (population 1,253,900), [Haifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haifa_metropolitan_area) (924,400), and [Beersheba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beersheba_metropolitan_area) (377,100).[^cite_note-643] The largest municipality, in population and area, is Jerusalem with 1,050,153 residents in an area of 125 square kilometres (48 sq mi).[^cite_note-Israelpopulations-644] Statistics on Jerusalem include the population and area of East Jerusalem, the status of which is in international dispute.[^cite_note-645] Tel Aviv and Haifa rank as Israel's next most populous cities, with populations of 494,900 and 297,082, respectively.[^cite_note-Israelpopulations-644] The (mainly [Haredi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haredi)) city of [Bnei Brak](314671.md) is the most densely populated city in Israel and one of the [10 most densely populated cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_proper_by_population_density) in the world.[^cite_note-646]&#x20;

Israel has 16 cities with populations over 100,000. As of 2018 there are 77 localities granted ["municipalities" (or "city") status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_council_\(Israel\)) by the Ministry of the Interior,[^cite_note-647] [four of which are in the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_settlements_with_city_status_in_the_West_Bank).[^cite_note-648]&#x20;
